# Corrections log

This log records factual corrections (misquotations, misattributions, wrong dates or citations, and internal
contradictions) made to ledger pages, one line per fix, with the source opened to verify it, so that each
correction can be checked and carried to other copies of the material.

- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption.html (John of Damascus entry): said Mary's tomb "had been opened at the Empress Pulcheria's request", contradicting the topic's own sources (the tomb opened at a late-arriving apostle's request; Marcian and Pulcheria asked Juvenal for her relics) → reworded to Juvenal's answer to Marcian and Pulcheria, the tomb opened at the late apostle's request (source: docs/doctrines/assumption/john-of-damascus.html and Holweck, Catholic Encyclopedia "The Feast of the Assumption", newadvent.org/cathen/02006b.htm, "upon the request of St. Thomas")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption/germanus-of-constantinople.html: Notes called Germanus a "seventh-century preacher", contradicting the page's own Dates line (c. 720; patriarch c. 715–730) → phrase removed; the note now reports only that Pius XII cited him in 1950 (source: the page's Dates line; Munificentissimus Deus, vatican.va, which cites Germanus, Sermo I)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption/pius-xii.html: "passed from this life" cited to MD §§5, 14; §5 does not contain it → cited to §14 ("had actually passed from this life"), with §17 ("passed from this earthly exile to heaven") (source: Munificentissimus Deus, vatican.va English text)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption/modestus-of-jerusalem.html: Notes said Mary's empty tomb was "opened at Chalcedon (451)"; the topic's sources place Juvenal's report at Chalcedon, not the opening → "Juvenal of Jerusalem's report, at Chalcedon (451), of Mary's empty tomb at Gethsemane" (source: docs/doctrines/assumption/john-of-damascus.html, Euthymiac History account)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption/john-of-damascus.html: internal contradiction — the page said John "rests the belief on tradition" and "grounds the doctrine not in the sacred text but in a chain of tradition" on the strength of the Euthymiac History excerpt, while also calling that excerpt a later interpolation; and the homily itself argues from scriptural figures → John's own text (fittingness; the Ark borne "to rest in the temple of the Lord") separated from the interpolated excerpt (source: John of Damascus, Homily II on the Dormition, malankaraworld.com English text)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/assumption/arguments/immortalist-translation.html (Sources, MD note): unsourced statement that the 1950 drafting avoided "death" "because the tradition was itself divided" → replaced by pointers to the two sourced accounts of the drafting (sources: John Paul II, General Audience 25 June 1997 §1, vatican.va; Shoemaker, Ancient Traditions (2002), p. 17)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/balthasar-hubmaier.html (Notes) and docs/doctrines/infant-baptism.html (Hubmaier entry): the page said "He did not retract" and the timeline "Unbowed to the end", but Vedder (already the page's main source) records his recantation at Zürich in 1525–26, its withdrawal, a further submission after the rack, and the January 1528 statement to Ferdinand that some biographers call a recantation → both now report the recantations and Vedder's note that he kept his view of the sacraments (source: Henry C. Vedder, Balthasar Hübmaier (1905), pp. 129, 137–141, 230, 236–237, archive.org balthasarhbmai00vedd; Philip Schaff, History of the Christian Church vol. 8 §26, ccel.org)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/schleitheim-confession.html (Summary and Notes): "the whole preceding tradition took infants to be fit subjects" and "the pivot from a timing question to a subject question" contradicted the timeline's own earlier entries (Peter of Bruys c. 1131, who held infant baptism worthless and re-baptized; Karlstadt and Reublin, 1524) → "the patristic witnesses on this timeline took infants to be fit subjects", and the subject question noted as raised by the Petrobrusians (source: docs/doctrines/infant-baptism.html Peter of Bruys entry, citing Peter the Venerable, Contra Petrobrusianos, via Schaff vol. 5)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/arguments/waldenses-anti-paedobaptism.html: called Thomas Armitage "the successionist", but the page's own cited source says Armitage held "a model of Baptist history which did not advocate for visible institutional succession of Baptist churches" → "the Baptist historian Thomas Armitage, who argued for Baptist principles throughout history without a visible succession of churches" (source: Wikipedia, "Baptist successionism", raw text)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/arguments/antiquity-universality.html: listed Newman's Essay on Development (1845) as a "theological cousin" of the apostolic-custom reading, holding the Church's consentient witness a guide to the apostolic deposit; Newman's own treatment of infant baptism (ch. 4 §1.3) instead argues that the duty of infant baptism "was less earnestly insisted on in early times" and became the rule only later → Newman given his own reading, quoted from the Essay (source: Newman, Essay on the Development of Christian Doctrine, 8th ed. 1891, pp. 59, 127–129, archive.org essayondevelopme00newmuoft)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/victor-of-rome.html and roman-primacy.html (Victor entry): stated that the threatened excommunication "did not stand", contradicting the page's own Notes that Eusebius does not say whether Victor's letters severed anyone → now says Eusebius does not record whether the sentence took effect, with Fortescue's view ("Victor intended to do it but did not") attributed (source: Eusebius, Church History V.24.9–18, NPNF, newadvent.org/fathers/250105.htm; A. Fortescue, The Early Papacy, ch. 5, archive.org)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/stephen-of-rome.html and roman-primacy.html (Stephen entry and fn): "Stephen's own words do not survive" → Cyprian, Epistle 73 (to Pompey) §1, quotes Stephen's ruling ("let nothing be innovated (or done) which has not been handed down…"); his Petrine claim survives only via Firmilian (source: Writings-Database Cyprian/Letters/Epistle 73.html, ANF vol. V)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/council-of-sardica.html and roman-primacy.html (Sardica entry): unfootnoted "its canons never entered the Eastern collections as Sardican" (and "authority outside the West was disputed from the start") → Quinisext (Trullo, 692) canon 2 sets its seal on the canons "of Sardica" (source: Writings-Database Quinisext Council/The Canons.html, canon II)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/stephen-of-rome.html fn 1: citation text garbled (the edition parenthesis pasted three times inside itself; same corruption in the pre-conversion page) → single clean citation (source: the page's own citation; Writings-Database Cyprian/Letters/Epistle 74.html ch. 6)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/arguments/clement-intervention-jurisdiction.html: J. B. Lightfoot listed (via McCue n. 6) under the reading that Rome speaks as superior to subject → Lightfoot's own text says the Romans "remonstrate on terms of equality" and calls the primacy "not of the episcopate, but of the church … not of official authority but of practical goodness" while calling the letter "the first step towards papal domination"; now filed under that reading with McCue's classification attributed (source: Lightfoot, The Apostolic Fathers I.1, 2nd ed. 1890, pp. 69–71, archive.org/details/p1apostolicfathe01clemuoft)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/stephen-of-rome.html (Notes, "Later reception"): sentence garbled (duplicated clause and broken entity text "ldquo;the first who held…rdquo;") → "names Agrippinus of Carthage as, in his words, “the first who held” that baptism ought to be repeated" (source: Vincent of Lérins, Commonitory ch. VI, Writings-Database "Vincent of Lérins/THE COMMONITORY/A Commonitory.html", as quoted in the page's own fn 6)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/stephen-of-rome.html (Notes, "Provenance"): "Cyprian's Epistle 75 to Pompey in some numberings" contradicted the page's own citation of the letter to Pompey as Ep. 73 → Firmilian's letter is Epistle 74 in the ANF collection and Epistle 75 in the CSEL numbering (source: J. Caminiti, Inventing Catholicity, DPhil thesis, Oxford 2025, n. 53 on CSEL numbering and n. 211 citing Firmilian as "Ep. 75.19.3", ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:f86fb133-2dc0-4754-ba44-82ea7d94b8fb)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/council-of-constantinople.html (Summary and Notes) and roman-primacy.html (Constantinople entry): unfootnoted claim that Rome "declined to receive canon 3 as law, objecting … to the civic principle behind it", placed at 381 → reported as Leo the Great's response in 451 (the canon "never brought to the knowledge of the Apostolic See"; the ranks of Alexandria and Antioch defended) (source: Leo, Letter 106 §V, Writings-Database "Leo the Great/Letters/Letter 106 To Anatolius….html")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/arguments/clement-intervention-jurisdiction.html: Emil Brunner listed under "An exercise of the primacy of the Roman Church"; McCue's n. 6 attaches him (with Lightfoot) only to the first of three points in Quasten's reading, that Rome writes "as a superior to a subject" → moved to the footnote as granting the tone, his view of the primacy not reported (source: J. F. McCue, Theological Studies 25 (1964) pp. 165–166 and n. 6, theologicalstudies.net PDF)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/arguments/clement-intervention-jurisdiction.html: Patrick Granfield and Peter C. Phan named (⚠ unverified) for the fraternal reading, though the page's own cited survey does not name them → removed (source: the page's own fn 9, earlychristianwritings.com "First Clement")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/arguments/cyprian-de-unitate-recensions.html, cyprian-of-carthage.html and roman-primacy.html (Cyprian entry): the third reading's conclusion ("the chair of Peter … not a title of the Roman see") was footnoted to Bévenot (unverified), and the "Primacy Text an earlier draft that makes Peter the source of a shared episcopate" attributed to him without a source opened → the conclusion attributed to Afanasiev; Bévenot limited to authorship (both versions Cyprian's, reversing Chapman's order), with his interpretation marked ⚠ unverified (sources: Caminiti, Inventing Catholicity (2025) n. 120; N. Afanasiev, "The Church Which Presides in Love", web.archive.org golubinski.ru text)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy.html (fn on the 1 Clement disputed reading): Schaff filed under two readings and Fortescue missing, contradicting the interpretation page → aligned with the page's Held-by lines (source: docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/arguments/clement-intervention-jurisdiction.html; Schaff, HCC vol. II §162, ccel.org)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy/victor-of-rome.html (Notes): stray "‡" glyph before epeirato → removed (source: the page's own fn 2, "The verb is 'attempted' (epeirato)")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/roman-primacy (all pages): Nicholas Afanasiev labelled "Russian Orthodox", implying he spoke for the Moscow Patriarchate → "Orthodox theologian, St Sergius Institute, Paris" (source: OrthodoxWiki, "Nicholas Afanasiev", orthodoxwiki.org/Nicholas_Afanasiev)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/augustine-of-hippo.html (Notes) and martin-luther.html (Notes): stated in the narrator's voice that Chrysostom "grounds their baptism in the sheer abundance of grace" (Luther page: an unattributed quoted "abundance of grace"), contradicting the Chrysostom page, which reports the reading of his "sinless" as disputed → the three readings attributed (Augustine; Wenger via Harkins; Meyendorff 1974) and the quoted phrase removed (source: docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/john-chrysostom.html and its cited Harkins, ACW 31, p. 233, and Meyendorff, Byzantine Theology, "Original Sin")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/infant-baptism/john-spilsbury.html and infant-baptism.html (Spilsbury entry): stated as fact that Spilsbury "held the covenant theology of the Reformed — one covenant of grace", a characterization the page's own note draws from Renihan → attributed to James M. Renihan ("approved covenant theology") (source: the page's footnote 3, quoting Renihan, "John Spilsbury," in Haykin, ed., The British Particular Baptists 1638–1910, 1:22–33)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/great-apostasy/the-historians-of-doctrine.html: said Harnack "even holds that catholic Christianity preserved the faith from being hellenized to the utmost", a paraphrase not found in the cited History of Dogma vol. 1, Book I, ch. 4 → replaced with Harnack's own words from that chapter ("the Catholic system… represents a gradual process of the same kind"; the traditional religion "was yet vigorous enough to reject that picture"; "Gnosticism… has in Catholicism obtained half a victory") (source: CCEL, ccel.org/ccel/harnack/dogma1.ii.iii.iv.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/great-apostasy.html (historians of doctrine entry): the quotation "theological explication was to a large extent left unfettered" was attributed jointly to Kelly and Pelikan → attributed to Kelly alone, Pelikan reported by his title (source: the topic's own footnote and great-apostasy/the-historians-of-doctrine.html, which cite it to Kelly, Early Christian Doctrines, ch. 1)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations.html (Jerome entry) and docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/jerome.html (Summary): presented "has not rendered his original word for word… has given the sense in different terms" as Jerome's answer for the Evangelist at Mark 1:2, and credited Jerome with the principle that "a blended quotation is fairly headed with the name of its senior or governing prophet"; in Letter 57 §9 those words are said of Paul's citation at 1 Cor 2:9, Jerome leaves the Mark question to his critics ("Let ignorant presumption solve this nice question if it can"), and the senior-prophet principle is not in Letter 57 or his Commentary on Matthew → reattributed and removed (source: Jerome, Letter 57, Writings-Database Jerome/Letters/letter_057.html, NPNF2 vol. 6; Jerome, Commentary on Matthew, Writings-Database)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/isaiah-malachi-composite.html and docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/jerome.html fn 2: quoted "to give sense for sense and not word for word" (§5) as Jerome's account of the Evangelists' citing method; the phrase is the NPNF editor's summary, and Jerome's own §5 ("I render sense for sense and not word for word") describes his translating from the Greek "except in the case of the holy scriptures" → corrected (source: Letter 57, Writings-Database, NPNF2 vol. 6)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/isaiah-malachi-composite.html fn 5: a Bede quotation ("It may be, too, that Isaiah occurred to the mind of Mark… as often happens…") was cited to Bede's Commentary on Mark at the local-text link, but its wording is the Catena Aurea's English rendering of Bede → the page now quotes the linked Commentary on Mark text and notes the Catena rendering (source: Writings-Database Bede/Commentary on Mark.html; Thomas Aquinas/Catena Aurea/Commentary on Mark/Chapter 1.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/isaiah-malachi-composite.html: listed "the papyrus P76" among the witnesses to "in Isaiah the prophet" at Mark 1:2, citing James Snapp's catalogue, which lists Papyrus Oxyrhynchus 5073 and no P76 → replaced by the witnesses Snapp lists (source: J. Snapp Jr., "Mark 1:2 — In Isaiah, or In the Prophets?", thetextofthegospels.com, 2017)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations.html (Irenaeus entry) and docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/ambrosiaster.html (Notes): stated that Irenaeus's second-century text "already carries the general 'in the prophets' rather than the singular 'in Isaiah'"; Irenaeus also quotes Mark as beginning "as it is written in Esaias the prophet" (Against Heresies III.11.8) → both forms reported (source: Writings-Database Irenaeus/Irenaeus Against Heresies Book 3.html, ANF vol. 1, III.10.5 and III.11.8)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/testimonia-source.html: the blockquote presented as J. Rendel Harris's words was, by the page's own footnote, a paraphrase of his thesis "rather than a single continuous sentence" → replaced with a verbatim passage from Testimonies I (1916), ch. 6, p. 56 (source: archive.org p1testimoni00harruoft, full text)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/testimonia-source.html fn 1: placed the chapter "Spoken by Jeremy the Prophet" and the chapter on Irenaeus in volume 2 of Testimonies; both are in Part I (1916), chs. 6–7 → corrected (source: tables of contents, archive.org p1testimoni00harruoft and testimonies00harruoft)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations/arguments/isaiah-for-asaph-slip.html: Hort's "It is difficult not to think Ἠσαίου genuine" was printed with an epsilon-dasia (Ἑσαίου) → Ἠσαίου (source: Hort's note as quoted in the Pulpit Commentary on Matthew 13:35, biblehub.com/commentaries/matthew/13-35.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/conflated-citations.html fn 2 (Irenaeus): "On the manuscript split, see note 9" pointed to the Bede note → "note 11" (source: the page's own footnote numbering)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Trent entry and fn) and communion-one-kind/council-of-trent.html: the 1564 chalice indult was said to go to "Austria, Bohemia, and much of the Empire", to be "withdrawn diocese by diocese" and "last suppressed in Bohemia in 1621", and (Trent page) Mass to be celebrated sub utraque at Prague cathedral, all cited to the Catholic Encyclopedia, which instead says Pius IV "authorized some German bishops" and the concession "was withdrawn in the following year" → brief of 16 April 1564 to the subjects of the emperor and of Duke Albrecht of Bavaria, the Archbishop of Prague to empower priests to give both kinds, and the withdrawal reported in Toner's and MacCaffrey's words (sources: P. Toner, "Communion under Both Kinds," Catholic Encyclopedia 1908, newadvent.org/cathen/04175a.htm; Wikipedia "Antonín Brus of Mohelnice"; J. MacCaffrey, History of the Catholic Church from the Renaissance to the French Revolution, vol. I ch. IV, historion.net)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/council-of-basel.html (Notes): the 1564 indult "withdrawn again within a decade", contradicting the source the timeline cites → "withdrawn in the following year" per Toner (source: Catholic Encyclopedia, "Communion under Both Kinds", newadvent.org/cathen/04175a.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Basel fn) and communion-one-kind/council-of-basel.html: "had already, on 23 December 1436, acknowledged that communion under both kinds is not heretical", cited to the Catholic Encyclopedia "Hussites", which does not contain it, and placed before the 1437 ratification → the council ratified the Compacts on 15 January 1437 and acknowledged "only on 23 December" that communion under both kinds was not heretical (source: Wikipedia "Compacts of Basel", citing Stieber 1978 p. 163; CE "Hussites", newadvent.org/cathen/07585a.htm, checked)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Apostolic Constitutions entry) and communion-one-kind/apostolic-constitutions.html: called the Constitutions "the earliest surviving text to name the deacon as the cup's minister", contradicted by the timeline's own Cyprian entry (On the Lapsed 25, c. 251: "the deacon began to offer the cup to those present") → claim removed; Cyprian's deacon noted (source: docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/cyprian-of-carthage.html, quoting On the Lapsed 25)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Ambrose entry): called Ambrose "the earliest full Latin witness that both-kinds reception was the norm", contradicted by the timeline's own Latin witness Cyprian of Carthage (c. 251) → claim removed (source: the timeline's Cyprian entry and cyprian-of-carthage.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Cyril entry) and communion-one-kind/cyril-of-jerusalem.html: presented "giving heed lest thou lose any portion thereof" as Cyril's care against losing the Precious Blood, but the page's own quotation applies it to the Body received in the palm ("receive the Body of Christ … partake of it; giving heed lest thou lose any portion thereof") → described as care over the Body (source: the page's quotation of Catechetical Lecture 23 §21, NPNF, newadvent.org/fathers/310123.htm as cited there)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/council-of-constance.html: said the denial of one-kind communion was "the very charge under which Jan Hus would be burned" → the council's sentence on Hus (Session 15, 6 July 1415) does not mention the chalice and condemns his teaching and defence of Wyclif's condemned errors (source: Tanner, Decrees of the Ecumenical Councils, Council of Constance, papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum16.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/arguments/gelasius-reversal-of-discipline.html: the Augsburg Confession XXII quotation (passage and fn 1) set "Pope Gelasius commands…" before "And this usage has long remained…" behind an ellipsis, reversing the source's order → quoted in source order (source: Augsburg Confession XXII, bookofconcord.org)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/gelasius-i.html: quoted the Augsburg Confession as "Gelasius commands that the sacrament not be divided" → "Pope Gelasius commands that the Sacrament be not divided"; and said the Catholic reply read Gelasius of "priests concelebrating", while Aquinas's reply speaks of priests who "consecrate the entire sacrament" → Aquinas quoted (sources: bookofconcord.org Augsburg Confession XXII; Summa Theologiae III q. 80 a. 12 ad 1, newadvent.org/summa/4080.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind.html (Jakoubek entry): "For a thousand years no one had deliberately set out to give the withdrawn cup back" (unsourced) → removed; the Catholic Encyclopedia reports a Prague priest preaching the necessity of both kinds as far back as 1380 (source: CE "Hussites", newadvent.org/cathen/07585a.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/martin-luther.html and communion-one-kind.html (Luther entry, fn): quoted Calvin as calling communion in one kind "an invention of Satan"; the phrase is the section heading of the Beveridge translation, not Calvin's text → Calvin quoted from IV.17.47 ("snatched or robbed a half of the Supper from the greater part of the people of God"), the heading attributed to Beveridge (source: Institutes IV.17.47, ccel.org/ccel/calvin/institutes.vi.xviii.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/image-veneration.html (Calvin entry): quoted Calvin as "God's majesty is defiled by an unseemly and absurd fiction", which is not the wording of the cited Beveridge translation (the page's own footnote quoted it correctly) → "the majesty of God is defiled by an absurd and indecorous fiction" (source: Calvin, Institutes I.11.2, Beveridge trans., ccel.org/ccel/calvin/institutes.iii.xii.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/image-veneration.html (Eusebius entry): quoted Eusebius as saying the ancients honoured the likenesses "without reserve"; the cited NPNF translation says "according to a habit of the Gentiles, to pay this kind of honor indiscriminately" → quotation corrected to the cited translation (source: Eusebius, Church History VII.18.4, NPNF2 vol. 1, newadvent.org/fathers/250107.htm and Writings-Database "Eusebius of Caesarea/THE CHURCH HISTORY OF EUSEBIUS/Book 7.html")
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/image-veneration.html (Eusebius entry) and arguments/early-church-aniconic.html: said the Letter to Constantia "survives only as quoted by the later iconoclasts" / "only inside the later iconoclast dossier"; it survives in the sections of the Horos of 754 read out at Nicaea II, with further fragments in the iconophile patriarch Nikephoros → reworded accordingly (source: D. M. Gwynn, "From Iconoclasm to Arianism", GRBS 47 (2007), p. 227, grbs.library.duke.edu PDF)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/image-veneration/synod-of-frankfurt.html (fn 2): cited the Orthodox Arts Journal (P. Brooke, 2017) for the translator's "word-by-word rendering" from Greek–Latin glossaries, which that article does not state (it reports the Constantine of Constantia rendering and calls the translation "very bad") → the glossary method marked ⚠ unverified in the text and the footnote says the source does not state it (source: orthodoxartsjournal.org article, opened)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/christian-humanism/arguments/donation-of-constantine.html: placed Corbie Abbey "among the proposed places of manufacture" within the eighth-century dating "in the orbit of Pope Stephen II and Pepin the Short"; the cited source ties the Corbie proposal to a ninth-century dating (Johannes Fried, 2007) and separately reports the Rome/752–795 and Frankish/St-Denis 840–850 placements → the placements are now given separately, each attributed (sources: Wikipedia, "Donation of Constantine", raw text; J. P. Kirsch, "Donation of Constantine", Catholic Encyclopedia 1909, newadvent.org/cathen/05118a.htm)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/christian-humanism/lorenzo-valla.html, arguments/donation-of-constantine.html and christian-humanism.html (fn donation-summary-2): Valla's occasion given as Alfonso "at war with Pope Eugenius IV over the Kingdom of Naples, a realm the papacy claimed the right to dispose of partly on the strength of the Donation", cited to the Hanover College introduction, which says only that Valla was "secretary to Alfonso, king of Aragon, Sicily, and Naples" and that the treatise "may well be considered as part of the campaign which that king was conducting against Pope Eugenius IV in furtherance of his claims to Italian territories" → replaced by that wording, attributed to Coleman; the composition date "2 April–25 May 1440" is not in that introduction and is now marked ⚠ unverified (source: C. B. Coleman's introduction, history.hanover.edu/texts/vallaintro.html)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/christian-humanism.html (Reuchlin entry): "Here the sources-quarrel reached the Hebrew fountain and, for the first time, drew blood" contradicted the page's own Pico entry, which records the condemnation of Pico's theses in 1487 → sentence removed; the Pico entry now notes it is the earliest condemnation on the timeline (source: the page's Pico entry and fn pico-summary-3, Kristeller in The Renaissance Philosophy of Man, 1948)
- 2026-11-17 docs/doctrines/christian-humanism.html (Reuchlin entry): the phrase "could not read the languages they wished to burn" was set in quotation marks as if quoted from the Letters of Obscure Men, but it is the person page's own paraphrase of the satire's joke → quotation removed and the sentence rewritten (source: docs/doctrines/christian-humanism/johann-reuchlin.html, "The satire's standing joke is that Reuchlin's enemies cannot even read the languages they wish to burn")

- 2026-11-18 apostle-philip/polycrates.html: daughters quoted as "grew old as virgins", not the wording of the cited NPNF translation → "two aged virgin daughters" (source: HE 3.31.3, NPNF, Writings-Database Book 3)

## death-of-judas (review fixes, 2026-11-18)
- `death-of-judas/modern-scholarship.html`: Lake's Latin gloss misattributed. The page called *et suspensus crepuit medius* "the Old Latin gloss" in Augustine's text. Lake, *Beginnings of Christianity* V, 26–27 (archive.org text, opened): the gloss is *collum sibi alligavit* in Augustine's African Latin (*Contra Felicem*), "an attempt to harmonize Acts with Matthew"; Jerome knew it in the shorter form *suspensus*, which the Vulgate reads (*suspensus crepuit medius*). Corrected.
- `death-of-judas.html`, `oecumenius.html`: dropped the claim that Oecumenius is the earliest statement of the harmony as a mechanism; Apollinaris (c. 382) already gives one (cut down alive). Oecumenius heading now notes Moss's late-tenth-century dating.
- `papias-of-hierapolis.html`: Dates line said the Judas notice "is quoted c. 110"; c. 110 is the date of the *Exposition*, the notice survives in later quotation. "Within living memory of the apostolic age" replaced by Papias's own statement as Eusebius quotes it (*HE* 3.39.4, newadvent, opened).
- `papias-swelling-tradition.html`, `theophylact-of-ohrid.html`: "neither Gospel" / "both Gospels" → canonical notices (Acts is not a Gospel).
- Lake (1933) refiled from "independent traditions" to one loose traitor-death tradition without literary dependence (his own words, Beginnings V); timeline, modern-scholarship page and index paragraph.
- 2026-11-18 `discernment-of-dreams/athanasius-of-alexandria.html`: Notes called Tertullian ("the soul itself apparently creates for itself") one of the "later writers" relative to Athanasius, but the page's own timeline dates Tertullian c. 210 and Athanasius c. 318 → note rewritten to list the natural-source witnesses by their dates (source: the topic's own timeline and Tertullian page, *De Anima* 47).
- 2026-11-18 `discernment-of-dreams/john-cassian.html`: called Cassian's three origins (c. 426) "Tertullian's and Gregory's taxonomy transposed"; Gregory the Great's six kinds date c. 593, after Cassian, and Gregory of Nyssa's page states his frame is two-source, not the God/devil/self scheme → comparison limited to Tertullian (c. 210) (source: the topic's own timeline dates and gregory-of-nyssa.html Notes).
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/death-of-moses/fathers-on-the-death.html (and timeline, fathers-death-1): the Ambrose blockquote ("a translation rather than his demise… transferred rather than abandoned") did not match the linked source, whose text is Savage's translation ("taken up into heaven rather than buried… translated rather than left here") → blockquote, prose and footnotes replaced with Savage's text verbatim, translator credited (source: Ambrose, On Cain and Abel I.2.8, trans. J. J. Savage, FOTC 42, the linked historicalchristian.faith text)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/death-of-moses/arguments/jude-source-and-canon.html: the Didymus quotation, elided as some "object to the Assumption of Moses… on account of that place", dropped the words naming the letter itself ("praesenti epistolae et Moyseos Assumptioni") while the page said Jude was the object → Didymus given in Charles's Latin in full (source: R. H. Charles, The Assumption of Moses (1897), p. 108, archive.org text)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/death-of-moses.html (Origen entry): "not held in the canon" was attached to the On First Principles citation of The Ascension of Moses; Origen says it of the "little book" in Homilies on Joshua 2.1 → moved to the homily (source: the topic's own quotations of both works; Charles, Assumption, p. 108)

- 2026-11-18 nephilim.html: Bauckham listed flatly as holding the Watcher reading of 1 Peter 3:19, which new-testament.html marks ⚠ unverified → marked ⚠ unverified on the timeline (source: new-testament.html)
- 2026-11-18 nephilim.html: footnotes on 2 Peter's "Tartarus" filed Neyrey and Billings under Bauckham's Jewish-mediation reading, and on the Titanomachy filed Heiser under the "Greek myth remembers the Watchers" reading → aligned with the interpretation pages' Held-by lines (source: 2-peter-tartarus-titanomachy.html, watchers-maccabean-allegory.html)

- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-name/arguments/yahweh-reconstruction.html: footnote quoted Maas as "there is no trace in Hebrew of a hiphil form of the verb meaning 'to be'" → corrected to the article's wording "nor is there any trace in Hebrew of a hiphil form of the verb meaning 'to be'" (source: Maas, "Jehovah (Yahweh)," Catholic Encyclopedia, newadvent.org/cathen/08329a.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-name/arguments/nt-tetragrammaton.html: footnotes swapped their content — Marlowe's "The New World Translation" was cited for the 237 New Testament insertions and reliance on Hebrew "J documents" (14th century onward), which that page does not contain, and Metzger (Theology Today 1953) for "Jehovah only where the Father is meant," which he does not say → Metzger now cited for the 237 passages and for the NWT footnotes' Hebrew New Testament translations "made in the sixteenth and succeeding centuries"; Marlowe for "Jehovah" given for kurios "whenever the Father is meant, but not when it refers to Christ" (sources: bible-researcher.com/metzger.jw.html; bible-researcher.com/new-world.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-name/arguments/nt-tetragrammaton.html (and timeline Disputed readings): said the New World Translation rests its New Testament "Jehovah" on Howard's 1977 reconstruction, but its New Testament first appeared in 1950 → claim removed; any later appeal to Howard marked ⚠ unverified (source: Marlowe, "The New World Translation," bible-researcher.com/new-world.html, on the 1950 first edition)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-council.html and divine-council/justin-martyr.html: attributed "the gods of the nations, reputed gods, are idols of demons, and not gods" (Dialogue with Trypho LV) to Justin as his own statement, but the words are Trypho's → reported as Trypho's citation, which Justin, replying, does not dispute ("aware those who worship these [idols] and such like are condemned") (source: Justin, Dialogue with Trypho LV, Writings-Database/Justin Martyr/Dialogue with Trypho.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-council.html and divine-council/origen-of-alexandria.html: quoted "the various quarters of the earth were from the beginning allotted to different superintending spirits…" as Origen's own words, but it is Celsus's, quoted by Origen at Against Celsus V.25 → attributed to Celsus; Origen's own phrasing (V.26 "who it was that distributed…", V.28 "the original distribution of the various quarters of the earth among different superintending spirits") added (source: Origen, Against Celsus V.25–28, Writings-Database/Origen of Alexandria/Against Celsus/Book 5.html)

- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/divine-name/arguments/nt-tetragrammaton.html: the New World Translation committee was filed as holding that the Name stood in the New Testament autographs, but neither cited source (Metzger 1953; Marlowe) reports the committee stating that thesis → marked ⚠ unverified, with the footnote saying what the sources do show (sources: https://www.bible-researcher.com/metzger.jw.html; https://www.bible-researcher.com/new-world.html)

- 2026-11-18 friendship/ambrose-of-milan.html: garbled sentence tying the false-witness rule to "when one knows of any fault in a friend" (which is III.22.127 on rebuke) → restated from III.22.126: Scripture "blames not witness given against a friend, but false witness"; where God's or country's cause compels witness, friendship gives way (source: De Officiis III.22.126, NPNF text)
- 2026-11-18 friendship/arguments/faith-not-virtue.html + virtue-friendship-transfer.html: Aquinas misquoted as "a certain friendship of man for God" and an unverified gloss ("a supernatural friendship added/laid above the natural") stated as fact → "the friendship of man for God" (q.23 a.1) plus the verified q.25 a.6 "in respect of their nature"; gloss and ⚠ footnote note removed (source: newadvent.org Summa II-II q.23, q.25)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/genealogies-of-jesus/john-of-damascus.html: said John "substitutes 'Mathan' and 'Melchi'" and adds Levi/Panther "matching the received text of Luke" — Africanus already names Matthan and Melchi, and John's Levi→Melchi order does not match Luke 3:23–24 (Heli–Matthat–Levi–Melchi) → now says only that John names Levi as Melchi's father and Panther as his brother, which Africanus's letter does not (source: Africanus, Epistle to Aristides, ANF VI text and editor's note 11, Writings-Database; John of Damascus, Orthodox Faith 4.14 as quoted on the page)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/genealogies-of-jesus/augustine-of-hippo.html (+ timeline entry and its note 19): stated as fact that in the Retractations Augustine "no longer excluded" the levirate / "allowed" a levirate sense, with no source for the paraphrase (the cited ANF note only says "consult… Augustine, Retract., ii. 7") → attributed to McCarthy's report that Augustine "withdrew this theory in favor of the theory of levirate marriage"; ANF cross-reference quoted as printed ("Augustine, Retract., ii. 7", was "Retract. ii. 7") (source: ANF VI note to Epistle to Aristides, Writings-Database; McCarthy, Living Tradition 11, rtforum.org/lt/lt11.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/goliath-slayer.html (note 2) + goliath-slayer/arguments/scribal-correction.html (+ merged-away elhanan-original-slayer): "most scholars dismiss the later 1 Chronicles 20:5 material as 'an obvious harmonization'" attributed to Azzan Yadin, "Goliath's Armor and Israelite Collective Memory," VT 54 (2004) → Wikipedia cites the sentence to Robert L. Hubbard et al., NIVAC Bundle 2: Historical Books (Zondervan, 2015) ("Most scholars dismiss the parallel in 1 Chronicles 20:5 as an obvious harmonization"); Yadin is cited there only for Goliath's armour; now attributed to Hubbard et al. as quoted at Wikipedia, ⚠ unverified (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goliath wikitext)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/goliath-slayer/targum-jonathan.html + goliath-slayer.html (Targum entry): said the Targum "makes explicit the identification Josephus had only implied" (Elhanan is David) → Josephus calls Elhanan "Nephan his kinsman," a relative of David, not David; now says the Targum identifies Elhanan with David (source: Josephus, Antiquities 7.301–302, Whiston, as quoted on goliath-slayer/josephus.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/goliath-slayer/arguments/scribal-correction.html: said Archer's three-slip reconstruction is "summarized at GotQuestions" → GotQuestions does not name Archer; Archer is cited (Encyclopedia of Bible Difficulties, p. 179) by Matt Slick on the followintruth.com page reproducing Heiser's essay; now cited there, ⚠ unverified (source: gotquestions.org/Goliath-David-Elhanan.html; followintruth.com/who-killed-goliath-david-or-elhanan)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/goliath-slayer.html (intro): said "nearly every serious reader — ancient rabbi, Reformation Hebraist, modern text-critic alike — agrees" the Masoretic 2 Sam 21:19 suffered in copying, contradicted by the page's own Targum and Rashi entries, which keep the consonants and read them as epithets for David → scoped to the commentators from Kennicott onward (source: the page's own Targum Jonathan and Rashi citations, Sefaria)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/goliath-slayer/josephus.html: said "the Hebrew text history" inserts "the brother of" into 2 Sam 21:19 → the insertion is in translations (KJV, NIV), the NIV noting "the Hebrew does not have the brother of" (source: NIV and ESV notes on 2 Sam 21:19 as cited on goliath-slayer.html note 1; ESV opened)
- 2026-11-18 goliath-slayer.html: intro said the commentators from Kennicott onward read all the Hebrew differences as scribal slips, contradicting the page's own Driver ("obvious" deliberate correction) and Smith ("harmonistic purpose") entries → slips attributed to Kennicott, Keil & Delitzsch, Heiser and Ozoliņš; Driver and Smith described as reading the Chronicles side as deliberate harmonization (source: the page's own Driver and Smith quotations)
- 2026-11-18 goliath-slayer/michael-heiser.html: Notes contrasted Heiser's "scribal accident" with Kennicott/Keil & Delitzsch's supposed "deliberate emendation", but Kennicott ("Mistake of some Transcriber") and Keil & Delitzsch ("an error in copying") also posit accident → clause replaced with "and, like them, explains it as a copying accident" (source: Kennicott and Keil & Delitzsch detail-page quotations)
- 2026-11-18 goliath-slayer (summary, scribal-correction, michael-heiser): "a shared original naming Goliath's unnamed brother" was self-contradictory → "a shared original in which Elhanan killed Goliath's unnamed brother" (source: Ozoliņš detail page)
- 2026-11-18 goliath-slayer/arguments/elhanan-is-david.html: Held by listed pseudo-Jerome's Quaestiones without qualification, though its Chronicles note makes Elhanan a separate man → qualified "in its note on 2 Samuel 21:19 (its note on Chronicles reads otherwise)" (source: pseudo-Jerome detail page, PL 23 col. 1385 note)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/arguments/sons-of-balaam.html: "full of contradictions and impossibilities and anachronisms" attributed to the Jewish Encyclopedia (1906) → attributed to the International Standard Bible Encyclopedia (1915) (source: ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/; phrase absent from Kohler, Jewish Encyclopedia 1906, https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8513-jannes-and-jambres)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/arguments/fate-in-legend.html: "the traditions of their death are… given in a varying fashion", the proselyte strand and the Red Sea / golden-calf deaths attributed to the Jewish Encyclopedia and Encyclopaedia Judaica → attributed to the ISBE (1915) (source: ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/; absent from Kohler, Jewish Encyclopedia 1906, https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8513-jannes-and-jambres and Encyclopaedia Judaica at https://www.jewishvirtuallibrary.org/jannes-and-jambres)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/arguments/fate-in-legend.html: misquote "or during the slaughter connected with it" → "or during the slaughter connected with the name of Phinehas" (source: ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/damascus-document.html: n. 5 quoted the Encyclopaedia Judaica as saying "in the Damascus Document only Jannes is named, whereas the second brother's name appears in later traditions", which the article does not contain → replaced with its actual wording ("as 'Jannes and his brother'"; Pliny and Apuleius "mention the name of Jannes only") (source: Encyclopaedia Judaica at https://www.jewishvirtuallibrary.org/jannes-and-jambres)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/arguments/name-origin-variants.html: JE's "equally untenable" applied to Steiner, Geiger, Jastrow and Levy → JE applies it to Jastrow and Levy only (Steiner proposes a Hebrew origin, Geiger reads 'sons of Jambri' as Amorites); the "to oppose"/participle Mambres derivation attributed to JE → A. J. Maclean (Hastings DB 1909) and ISBE; JE gives Mambre = "the rebel" (source: Kohler, Jewish Encyclopedia 1906, https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8513-jannes-and-jambres; ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/modern-scholarship.html: Yoḥana/John and "to oppose" derivations attributed to the Jewish Encyclopedia and Encyclopaedia Judaica → Maclean (Hastings DB 1909) and ISBE (1915); neither encyclopedia gives them (source: ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/; Kohler, Jewish Encyclopedia 1906, https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8513-jannes-and-jambres; Encyclopaedia Judaica at https://www.jewishvirtuallibrary.org/jannes-and-jambres)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/medieval-reception.html: n. 2 credited the Acts of Peter and Paul notice to Hastings' Dictionary of the Bible → Hastings' Dictionary of the Apostolic Church (1916); the Dibelius–Conzelmann remark is not on the cited page and is now marked ⚠ unverified (source: ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres.html and docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres/medieval-reception.html: Red Sea and golden-calf death traditions listed as "gathered in" the Jewish Encyclopedia → JE gives only the Zohar death; the others credited to the ISBE (1915); unsupported "burned" removed from the list of endings (source: Kohler, Jewish Encyclopedia 1906, https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8513-jannes-and-jambres; ISBE 1915 and Hastings DB 1909/DAC 1916 at https://www.sermonindex.net/encyclopedia/j/jannes-and-jambres/)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jannes-and-jambres.html: the naming in 2 Timothy called "the first datable witness to that extra-biblical tradition", contradicting the page's own Damascus Document entry (c. 100 BC) → "the earliest extant witness to both names of the pair" (source: the page's own Damascus Document entry and its n. 3)

- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jethro.html (footnote, now in jethro/modern-scholarship.html fn 2): the quote "J names Moses' father-in-law as Reuel or Hobab, whereas E knows him as Jethro" was attributed to the Encyclopaedia Judaica → it is Stephen L. Harris, Understanding the Bible (2010), p. 69, as quoted by Wikipedia; the EJ instead says "Some modern scholars assign Hobab to the j source and Jethro to the e document" (source: "Jethro (biblical figure)," Wikipedia; EJ "Jethro" at encyclopedia.com, read 2026-11-18)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/job.html, job/septuagint-appendix.html, job/arguments/job-is-jobab.html: the Septuagint appendix's genealogy was described as making Job "a great-grandson of Esau" → "a grandson of Esau" (the appendix makes him son of Zare, "one of the sons of Esau", "the fifth from Abraam") (source: Brenton LXX Job 42:17c, https://biblehub.com/sep/job/42.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/job.html, job/septuagint-appendix.html: the appendix's "seam" was stated as making Job the "son" of Zerah against Genesis (Genesis 36:33 also calls Jobab son of Zerah) → restated as the appendix calling Zare "one of the sons of Esau" where Genesis 36:13 lists a Zerah as son of Reuel (source: Brenton LXX Job 42:17c; Genesis 36:13, 33)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/job.html, job/septuagint-appendix.html: "the identification is older than the Greek Job itself" / "scholars from Freudenthal onward have judged" the appendix dependent on Aristeas, stated as settled → attributed: Freudenthal held the additions used Aristeas; Kohler and Wendland thought the reverse possibly more likely and dated Aristeas after the translation of Job (source: Jewish Encyclopedia, "Aristeas the Historian", http://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/1764-aristeas-the-historian)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/job/arguments/job-is-jobab.html: footnote cited the Jewish Encyclopedia article "Job" (1906) "on the identification as a construction resting on the name", but that article does not mention Jobab → citation for that point removed; the Kohler–Wendland "Aristeas the Historian" article cited instead for the reading it does state (source: https://www.jewishencyclopedia.com/articles/8692-job, opened and searched)

- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jethro.html: intro called Jethro "outside the covenant and the line of Abraham", but Midian is Abraham's son by Keturah (and the page's own fn 11 gives Ibn Kathir's Abrahamic genealogy) → "outside Israel's covenant" (source: Genesis 25:1-2, KJV)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jethro.html: intro had Moses meet the priest himself at the well → Moses meets the seven daughters at the well and is invited into the household (source: Exodus 2:16-20, KJV)

- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/jethro/islamic-and-druze.html: Qur'anic father-in-law described as pasturing the flock Moses leads to the mountain of God (an Exodus 3:1 detail) → detail dropped from the Qur'anic description (source: Exodus 3:1 as cited on the page)

- 2026-11-18 doctrines/job/ephrem-the-syrian.html + doctrines/job.html: described the commentary's 248-year reckoning as a "doubling" of Job's years, contradicting the page's own quote ("increased by a half": 170 + 85, less 7) → described as the quote computes it (source: the page's quoted Commentary on Job 42:16)
- 2026-11-18 doctrines/job/ephrem-the-syrian.html + doctrines/job.html: unsourced claim that the 248-year total is "a Peshitta/Syriac reckoning" → removed; person page now notes the quote's 170 years match Septuagint Job 42:16 (source: Brenton LXX, https://biblehub.com/sep/job/42.htm)

- 2026-11-18 theophilus-chrysostom (timeline, theophilus-of-alexandria, arguments/herodias-sermon-against-eudoxia): Palladius said to have been "deposed at the Oak" → "the Oak took up his case" (cod. 59 records the synod proceeding to "the case of the bishops Heraclides and Palladius of Helenopolis" and Isaac naming him an Origenist, but no deposition of Palladius) (source: Photius, Bibliotheca cod. 59, tertullian.org/fathers/photius_03bibliotheca.htm)
- 2026-11-18 theophilus-chrysostom/arguments/chrysostom-fell-for-origenism: "Socrates dates the plot to the day of Chrysostom's consecration" → "from immediately after Chrysostom's consecration" (Socrates: "immediately after his ordination, was plotting his overthrow") (source: Socrates, Church History VI.5, NPNF2-02-11)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/arguments/subordination-of-the-son.html: Tertullian quoted as saying the Word was “made perfect Son” (phrase not in the text) → replaced with the verbatim ANF wording of Against Praxeas 5 and 7 (“This is the perfect nativity of the Word, when He proceeds forth from God”; “He became also the Son of God, and was begotten when He proceeded forth from Him”) (source: https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0317.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/augustine-of-hippo.html: Augustine's "men of advanced years, and even presbyters" was presented as a gibe at Tertullian echoing Jerome's "presbyter" → it concerns the disciples of Vincentius Victor's teaching; restated so (source: Augustine, On the Soul and Its Origin II.9, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/15082.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/augustine-of-hippo.html: "he remembers Tertullian, and fears being compelled…" given as an unattributed 19th-century summary → attributed to John Huber, Philosophie der Kirchenväter, as quoted by Schaff (source: Schaff, History of the Christian Church III §154, https://ccel.org/ccel/schaff/hcc3/hcc3.iii.xii.xxxviii.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/augustine-of-hippo.html: "sicut scripta eius indicant" presented as Augustine's only evidence for the founding of the sect → in the page's own quoted Latin it qualifies the teaching on the soul; restated (source: De haeresibus 86, https://www.tertullian.org/tertullianistae/de_haeresibus.htm, as quoted on the page). NB the timeline page carries the same reading.
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/augustine-of-hippo.html: note 2 said the surrender of the basilica to bishop Aurelius is "drawn from the notice itself", which does not name Aurelius → Aurelius detail marked ⚠ unverified (source: De haeresibus 86 Latin, https://www.tertullian.org/tertullianistae/de_haeresibus.htm, as quoted on the page)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/jerome.html: Tertullian said to contrast "us laymen" with the clergy (not his wording) → replaced with the verbatim "Are not even we laics priests?" (source: Tertullian, On Exhortation to Chastity 7, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0405.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/jerome.html: Jerome's 385 departure from Rome was folded into what "Christine Mohrmann showed" → Mohrmann's article does not mention it; her view is now quoted alone and the 385 departure stated separately (source: Mohrmann, "Saint Jerome and Saint Augustine on Tertullian", https://www.tertullian.org/articles/mohrmann_jerome_eng.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/jerome.html: body named Jerome's verb as "lapsus est" while the page's own note 1 prints the Latin "ad Montani dogma delapsus" → body now follows note 1 (source: the page's note 1, PL 23). NB the timeline page still says "lapsus est".
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus.html, tomb-of-jesus/melito-of-sardis.html: scholar misspelled "U. C. van Wahlde" (text and fn 9) → "U. C. von Wahlde" (source: Eerdmans/Google Books listings of Urban C. von Wahlde, *The Gospel and Letters of John*, 2010; Loyola University Chicago Academia.edu profile)
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus.html, tomb-of-jesus/eusebius-of-caesarea.html: Joan Taylor filed as flatly holding Christians knew where the tomb was buried → reports her "very possible" plus "even though no extant source ever mentions this", and that her conclusion states it (source: Taylor, NTS 44 (1998), https://biblearchaeology.org/research-articles/golgotha-a-reconsideration-of-the-evidence-for-the-sites-of-jesus-crucifixion-and-burial/)
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus.html, tomb-of-jesus/melito-of-sardis.html: Taylor's Melito reading reported without her stated condition → adds her "if Melito's plateia is, in fact, the Decumanus" (source: Taylor, NTS 44 (1998), https://biblearchaeology.org/research-articles/golgotha-a-reconsideration-of-the-evidence-for-the-sites-of-jesus-crucifixion-and-burial/)
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus/eusebius-of-caesarea.html: said the finding of the Cross is first reported by Egeria later in the century, contradicting the topic's true-cross-discovery page → reported by Cyril of Jerusalem (lectures c. 350; Letter to Constantius, 351), later by Egeria (source: the page's own cited Cyril texts on arguments/true-cross-discovery.html)
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus/arguments/true-cross-discovery.html: question section credited Cyril with describing the Constantinian excavation → Eusebius describes the excavation; Cyril venerated cross-wood said to have been "found" (source: the page's own quoted Cyril passages, fn 8)
- 2026-11-18 tomb-of-jesus.html: five data about the place ascribed to "the evangelists" though one is Hebrews 13:12 → "The Gospels, with Hebrews 13:12" and heading "The New Testament gives the setting" (source: the page's own fn 3, Hebrews 13:12)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/vocation-and-work.html: Paul entry said 2 Thess 3:10 was "the very words Benedict would later cite to bind his monks to handwork", and its footnote that Benedict cites the text at Rule ch. 48 → removed; ch. 48 appeals to "our forefathers and the Apostles" but does not quote 2 Thess 3:10 (source: Rule of St. Benedict ch. 48, Verheyen trans., https://ccel.org/ccel/benedict/rule/rule.l.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/vocation-and-work.html and vocation-and-work/leo-xiii.html: Rerum Novarum said to fill "the ninety-year gap between the Reformers and the modern voices" → removed; the pages' own dates put Calvin's Institutes (1559) more than three centuries before 1891, and the ninety years run from Rerum Novarum to Laborem Exercens (source: the pages' own Dates lines and cited encyclicals)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/vocation-and-work/benedict-of-nursia.html: Rule ch. 48 said to appeal "to Paul directly" (Paul page Notes) / to "the tentmaking apostle" → reworded; the Rule names "our forefathers and the Apostles", not Paul (source: Rule ch. 48, https://ccel.org/ccel/benedict/rule/rule.l.html)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy.html: said Augustine ascribes the Tertullianist group to Tertullian "from the writings (sicut scripta eius indicant)" → the phrase introduces the teaching on the soul; Augustine names no source for the founding (source: Augustine, De haeresibus 86, Latin and Muller trans., tertullian.org/tertullianistae/de_haeresibus.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/arguments/never-formally-left-the-church.html: case text, second reading and notes 4, 13 said Augustine identifies the founder from Tertullian's books (sicut scripta eius indicant) → Augustine names Tertullian as founder without citing a source; the phrase governs the soul teaching (source: same De haeresibus 86 page)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/arguments/corporeality-of-the-soul.html: Vincentius Victor called "a fellow traducianist", and Augustine said to have "inclined toward a propagated soul" → Victor held souls "not derived by propagation, but breathed by God afresh"; Augustine left the question open, as the timeline states (source: Augustine, On the Soul and Its Origin, newadvent.org/fathers/15081.htm)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy.html, tertullian-heresy/jerome.html: Jerome called "a writer against second marriage" / Against Jovinianus "arguing against second marriage" → Against Jovinianus discourages remarriage without condemning it ("I do not condemn second, nor third... eighth marriages") (source: Jerome, Against Jovinianus I.15, newadvent.org/fathers/30091.htm)
- 2026-11-18 tertullian-heresy/jerome.html, arguments/standard-biography.html, arguments/resentment-of-the-roman-clergy.html: "about 172 years after Tertullian's death", though the death date is unknown (pages date him c. 220; Jerome reports a "decrepit old age") → "more than a century and a half after Tertullian's death (c. 220)" (source: pages' own Jerome, De viris illustribus 53)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/arguments/standard-biography.html: "Barnes withdrew the jurist identification" implied he once held it → "Barnes rejects the identification with the jurist" (source: page's own n. citing Barnes 1971 ch. 1)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy.html: note 38 filed T. D. Barnes (1971) as a holder of the never-left reading, which the reading page's Held-by line omits → Barnes cited only for noting the late works report no synod, excommunication or founding, as on the reading page's n. 8 (source: never-formally-left-the-church.html n. 8; Powell 1975, tertullian.org)
- 2026-11-18 docs/doctrines/tertullian-heresy/arguments/kept-theology-denied-sainthood.html: Lactantius's theology characterized in the narrator's voice ("a devil paired as God's second 'son'") → attributed to the Divine Institutes' own wording ("another being" who "passed from good to evil") and Jerome's charge ("the subsistence of the Holy Spirit") (source: newadvent.org/fathers/07012.htm ch. 9; Jerome, Ep. 84.7, newadvent.org/fathers/3001084.htm)
- 2026-11-18 vocation-and-work.html: Sayers entry said "Writing in the second winter of the world war" for an address given 23 April 1942 → "Writing in 1942, during the Second World War" (source: the Sayers person page's own cited dating, "Why Work?", Eastbourne, 23 April 1942)
- 2026-11-18 vocation-and-work.html, vocation-and-work/john-paul-ii.html, vocation-and-work/leo-xiii.html: Laborem Exercens (14 Sept 1981) said to be issued "on" Rerum Novarum's ninetieth anniversary, which fell 15 May 1981 → "for the ninetieth anniversary" (source: the pages' own cited dates — Rerum Novarum 15 May 1891 per the vatican.va URL; John Paul II page's Notes)
- 2026-11-18 vocation-and-work/martin-luther.html: Notes asserted, unsourced, that the milkmaid line was Wingren's distillation "rather than a sentence Luther himself wrote" → reduced to "quoted as Luther's in Wingren, Luther on Vocation (1957)", marked ⚠ unverified for its primary location (source: none opened; claim withdrawn as unsourced)
- 2026-11-18 vocation-and-work/hebrew-scriptures.html: date range c. 15th–3rd c. BC labelled "on the traditional reckoning" though its lower end is a critical dating → "the range spans traditional and critical datings" (source: page's own range; internal mislabel)
- 2026-11-18 vocation-and-work/benedict-of-nursia.html: unsourced "nineteenth-century Benedictine tag" for ora et labora → "a later tag", matching the timeline (source: timeline page's own wording)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/papias-of-hierapolis.html: Summary quoted Eusebius as "a story of a woman accused of many sins before the Lord" (not the translation's wording) → verbatim "relates another story of a woman, who was accused of many sins before the Lord, which is contained in the Gospel according to the Hebrews" (source: the page's own NPNF blockquote of HE 3.39.17, newadvent.org/fathers/250103.htm)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/didymus-the-blind.html: Summary paraphrased Metzger as saying no Greek Father "so much as mentions" the passage, and put "let him who has not sinned cast the stone" in quotation marks → Metzger's actual "comments on", and Didymus's verbatim "He who has not sinned, let him take a stone and cast it" (source: Metzger as quoted in fn 2; the page's own Ehrman-translation blockquote)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/jerome.html: Greek of the scribal gloss in fn 2 garbled ("τὰἁμαρτίας") against the same Metzger quotation on the timeline → "τὰς ἁμαρτίας"; also dropped the claim that Jeremiah 17:13 "recurs… in the scribal gloss itself" (the gloss is "the sins of every one of them" and cites no Jeremiah) (source: Metzger, Textual Commentary 190, as quoted on the topic timeline, fn scribes-1)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/bonaventure.html: called "John added it after the Gospel was completed" Bonaventure's own "guess"; Bonaventure reports it from "some" ("Some say that it was not found in the ancient Greek books, because John added it…") → attributed to "some" as reported by him (source: Bonaventure, Commentary on John ch. 8, Writings-Database / historicalchristian.faith)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/didymus-the-blind.html (+ timeline intro, euthymius-zigabenus.html Notes): Didymus called "the earliest Greek writer known to retell the story"/"earliest Greek witness", contradicting the Papias entry (c. 110) → "earliest Greek writer whose own retelling of such a story survives", noting Papias's report survives only through Eusebius (source: the pages' own Eusebius HE 3.39.17 citations)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/didymus-the-blind.html: "Didymus's copy … survived only by accident … sixth- and seventh-century codices" (a 6th–7th-c. codex cannot be Didymus's own copy) → "survives in a sixth- or seventh-century papyrus codex found among those hidden at Tura" (source: page's own Notes and fn 1, Kramer–Krebber Tura-Papyrus ed.)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/papias-of-hierapolis.html + timeline: Papias stated as "a hearer of the presbyter John" in narrator voice → attributed: Irenaeus "a hearer of John"; Eusebius "a hearer of Aristion and the presbyter John" (source: Eusebius, HE 3.39.1, 3.39.7, NPNF2 1, Writings-Database Book 3 / newadvent 250103)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/euthymius-zigabenus.html + timeline: Euthymius's note quoted as "in the accurate copies, either not found or marked with an obelus, because these things seem spurious and added", an uncited rendering differing from the page's own cited one → Migne-based wording "in the more accurate copies, either not found, or marked through with an obelus, because they seem illegitimate and added" (source: PG 129 col. 1280 Latin as given in fn 1 / roger-pearse.com)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/augustine-of-hippo.html + arguments/pericope-authenticity.html: Augustine said to blame "copyists" → his own wording, "some men of slight faith, or rather, some hostile to the true faith" removing it "from their Scriptural texts" (source: De adulterinis coniugiis 2.6–7, Wilcox FC 27, as quoted on the page)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/jerome.html: "Aquinas ranks the Jeremiah-based reading" implied Jerome's Jer 17:13 → Aquinas ranks Ambrose's Jer 22 oracle below the "better opinion" and cites Jer 17:13 for his reading of the ground (source: thomas-aquinas.html quotation of Super Ioannem 8)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground/arguments/pericope-authenticity.html: Brown paraphrased as Didascalia using the story "as a well-known example" → restored his "presumably" and his caveat "(but not necessarily as Scripture)" (source: Brown, AB 29, 335, at bible-researcher.com/adult.html)
- 2026-11-18 writing-on-the-ground.html: heading "The modern reconstructions (19th–20th century)" covered John Lightfoot (17th c.) and McGrath (2021) → "(17th–21st century)" (source: the section's own fn sotah-1)

- 2026-11-24 treasury-of-merit.html: "A modern academic Orthodox treatment of the treasury of merit itself was not located" no longer accurate → removed; Pomazansky, Orthodox Dogmatic Theology, added (source: holytrinitymission.org/books/english/dogmatics_pomazansky.htm)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/augustine-of-hippo.html: inline Sermon 227 quote read "sanctified by the word of God" and "If you have received well", not matching the cited edition → "consecrated by the word of God" and "If you have received worthily" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/Sermons/Sermon 227.html, the page's cited link)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/augustine-of-hippo.html: Tractate 26 inline quotes ("O sacrament of mercy, O sign of unity, O bond of love! Whoso wishes to live, let him draw nigh, believe, be incorporated, that he may be quickened"; "would have us understand the society of His body, and His members, which is the Church") did not match the cited NPNF text → "O mystery of piety! O sign of unity! O bond of charity! … Let him draw near, let him believe; let him be embodied, that he may be made to live" and "would have this meat and drink to be understood as meaning the fellowship of His own body and members, which is the holy Church" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/TRACTATES ON JOHN/Tractate 26.html, the page's cited link)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/augustine-of-hippo.html: Sermon 272 phrase quoted as "be what they see" → "Be what you see, and receive what you are" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/Sermons/Sermon 272.html and the page's own blockquote)

- 2026-11-24 real-presence/cyprian.html: Summary called Cyprian "the first Latin witness on this timeline", but Tertullian (c. 211, Latin) precedes him on the real-presence timeline → sentence removed (source: docs/doctrines/real-presence.html and real-presence/tertullian.html)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/thomas-aquinas.html: body applied the quoted phrase "has not properly a subject" to the remaining accidents, though the page's own footnote 3 places it in ST III q.75 a.5 ad 4 as said of the change → reworded so the phrase is said of the change and the subsisting accidents are referred to III q.77 (source: the page's footnote 3, ST III q.75 a.5 / q.77 as cited)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/irenaeus.html: Notes said Irenaeus's consecratory language was "as already seen in Origen's 'becomes by prayer'", but Origen (c. 248) postdates Irenaeus (c. 180–189) → recast as a later dated parallel, Origen (c. 248) (source: Dates lines of irenaeus.html and origen.html)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/tertullian.html: Notes counted Tertullian, Cyril of Alexandria and Trent as "three witnesses on this page", but Cyril and Trent are timeline entries, not on the page → "later entries on the timeline" (source: docs/doctrines/real-presence.html timeline)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/filioque/arguments/creed-alteration.html: the Ephesus canon 7 blockquote spliced a tail ("these… the holy and ecumenical Synod decrees… to be deposed") onto the Toledo-cited wording, and that tail is not in the cited source → replaced with the canon's full NPNF text (source: Percival, NPNF² 14, Ephesus Canon VII, https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf214.x.xvi.x.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/filioque/arguments/creed-alteration.html: said the canon was aimed at a Nestorianizing creed "Charisius had produced for converts" → per Percival's notes the canon followed the reading of Charisius's libellum (which was written against Nestorius and approved) and of a Nestorian creed (source: Percival's notes to Ephesus Canon VII, https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf214.x.xvi.x.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/filioque/arguments/creed-alteration.html: the Chalcedon Definition citation linked npnf214.viii.viii.html, which is not a Chalcedon page → npnf214.xi.xiii.html (source: CCEL NPNF² 14, "The Definition of Faith of the Council of Chalcedon", https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf214.xi.xiii.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/filioque/third-council-of-toledo.html (and the timeline's Toledo entry): quoted Percival as saying the early users "very likely did not know they were adding anything", but that wording is not in his excursus → his actual sentence, "the Spanish Church was unaware that it had added to or changed the Constantinopolitan creed" (source: Percival, Historical Excursus, https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf214.ix.iv.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/prayer-to-saints.html: Aquinas quoted as "in the first way we offer prayer to God alone" → "On the first way we offer prayer to God alone" (source: Summa Theologiae II-II q.83 a.4, newadvent.org/summa/3083.htm)

- 2026-11-24 real-presence/arguments/augustine-sign-and-figure.html: Sermon 227 quoted as "That bread which you see on the altar, sanctified by the word of God, is the body of Christ" → "That Bread which you see on the altar, consecrated by the word of God, is the Body of Christ" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/Sermons/Sermon 227.html, the page's cited link)
- 2026-11-24 real-presence/arguments/augustine-sign-and-figure.html: Sermon 272 quoted as "one thing is seen, another understood" → "one thing is seen, another is understood" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/Sermons/Sermon 272.html, the page's cited link)

- 2026-11-24 real-presence.html: Augustine entry and footnote quoted Tractate 26 as "O sign of unity … let him draw nigh, believe, be incorporated, that he may be quickened" → "O sign of unity! … Let him draw near, let him believe; let him be embodied, that he may be made to live" (source: Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/TRACTATES ON JOHN/Tractate 26.html, the cited link)

- 2026-11-24 filioque.html: narrator named the pope of the synodal letter as Martin I while dating the letter c. 645–646, before Martin's pontificate (from July 649) → "a Roman synodal letter", the bracket attributed to the translator and Gilbert's "(or, perhaps, Pope Theodore I)" reported (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Martin_I; the bekkos blog post and its comments)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/christology.html: the words "a valid existence… avoiding the perception of it being merely an illusion" were quoted as if Leontius' own; they trace only to an auto-generated Academia.edu summary, not to Leontius or a named author → quotation marks removed and paraphrased (source: web search for the phrase; Forum Philosophicum page returned 404)
