- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arguments/ante-nicene-conditionalism.html and conditional-immortality.html (second-century entry): Edward Fudge was named with Froom as advancing the thesis that the ante-Nicene church was broadly conditionalist; Farrar's review reports (quoting Fudge, 3rd ed., p. 385) that Fudge treats the debate over Justin as unresolved and counts Tatian, Athenagoras and Tertullian among holders of unending punishment (Fudge's ch. 25 is titled "The Apologists: A Fire That Torments") → Fudge moved to a separate reading, marked as reported by Farrar (sources: blog.dianoigo.com/2019/02/review-of-fire-that-consumes-by-edward.html; contents of edwardfudge.com/wordpress/wp-content/uploads/2014/05/excerptTFTC3.pdf)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration/venantius-fortunatus.html (Summary): both Vexilla regis prodeunt and Pange lingua were said to be "still sung at the Good Friday veneration of the Cross", contradicting the page's own Reception note and the timeline; Vexilla regis belongs to Passiontide Vespers (its processional use on Good Friday is the procession of the Sacrament) and only Pange lingua/Crux fidelis accompanies the Adoration → summary now assigns Pange lingua to the Good Friday Adoration and Vexilla regis to Passiontide Vespers (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vexilla_regis_prodeunt; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pange_lingua_gloriosi_proelium_certaminis)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration.html (intro): the Good Friday chant was quoted unsourced as "we adore thy Cross, O Lord" → quoted in the current Roman Missal wording "We adore your Cross, O Lord" (Crucem tuam adoramus, Domine) and footnoted (source: churchlifejournal.nd.edu/articles/music-of-holy-week-good-friday/; catholicculture.org/culture/library/view.cfm?id=3515 §§58, 68–69)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/arguments/indulgences-origin.html: Sixtus IV's application of indulgences to the dead "per modum suffragii (the Church having no jurisdiction over the dead)" was cited to Schaff, History of the Christian Church VI, which says instead that per Aquinas such souls "belong to the jurisdiction of the Church on earth" → the per modum suffragii / no-jurisdiction statement is now attributed to W. Kent, "Indulgences," Catholic Encyclopedia (1910), and Schaff's contrary statement reported as his (source: newadvent.org/cathen/07783a.htm; ccel.org/ccel/schaff/hcc6.iii.x.ix.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/arguments/indulgences-origin.html: History of the Christian Church vol. VI cited as by "P. Schaff" → David S. Schaff, the volume's author (source: ccel.org/ccel/schaff/hcc6/hcc6.i.html title page)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/arguments/prayer-for-the-dead-uncontested.html: "merely epitomises Epiphanius" attributed to "Henry Wace" → the article "Aerius" signed E.V. in Wace and Piercy's Dictionary of Christian Biography (1911), which Wace co-edited (source: ccel.org/ccel/wace/biodict.html?term=Aerius,+founder+of+the+heretical+sect+of+the+Aerians)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration.html (Calvin entry + its footnote): Institutes I.12.2 quoted with an ellipsis dropping "to angels and dead men", so the dulia/latria distinction read as invented to cover honours paid to the Cross → restored "permitting divine honours to be paid to angels and dead men with apparent impunity" (source: ccel.org/ccel/calvin/institutes.iii.xiii.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration.html (intro): said the faithful "kneel before" the Cross, a gesture no cited source names (Paschalis Sollemnitatis §65 has kneeling only in silent prayer at the entrance) → "bow to it and kiss it", footnoted to Egeria §37 and Paschalis Sollemnitatis §§69, 71 (sources: ccel.org/ccel/mcclure/etheria; catholicculture.org/culture/library/view.cfm?id=3515)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration/second-council-of-nicaea.html: unsourced "⚠ unverified: that the iconoclast party of the eighth century also honoured the Cross" → reported what the source says: the 730 edict "did not apply to ... religious symbols such as the cross" and the Chalke Gate image replaced with a cross (classic account; Leo III's removal called "fictitiously claimed" in the same article); "honoured" dropped (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Iconoclasm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cross-veneration/venantius-fortunatus.html: unsourced "The closing stanza now sung with the Crux fidelis is a later addition" → attributed and narrowed to the sources' wording, the hymn's "concluding stanza was not written by Fortunatus, but was added later" (sources: preces-latinae.org/thesaurus/Hymni/PangeF.html; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pange_lingua)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arguments/arnobius-patristic-witness.html: McCracken was quoted via Froom as "For Arnobius the Soul can only be mortal and he appears of the opinion that this view is orthodox Christianity"; his own text reads "the soul" and "appears to be of the opinion" → quoted from McCracken directly, Froom's wording kept in the footnote; McCracken also filed under the second reading for his own account of Arnobius's divergences (source: McCracken, Arnobius of Sicca: The Case Against the Pagans, ACW 7, 1949, Introduction, 20, 30, 33-34, archive.org/stream/ancientchristian009983mbp; Froom vol. 1, 920)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality.html (second-century entry) and arguments/ante-nicene-conditionalism.html: Florovsky was credited with Salmond's argument from Justin's First Apology and "everlasting punishment", and the reading-2 summary said "the wicked are punished without end" for all its holders; Florovsky's essay cites no First Apology and argues only creaturely contingency ("May cease" does not mean "will cease") → Salmond and Florovsky split; summary now says the soul "continues in being by God's will" (source: Florovsky, "The 'Immortality' of the Soul", fatheralexander.org/booklets/english/immortality_soul.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arguments/ante-nicene-conditionalism.html: the International Theological Commission (1990) was listed as a holder of the reading that the apologists taught the soul's creatureliness compatibly with unending existence, but §4.2 counts Tatian's thnetopsychism as an exception to the soul's survival and the document does not discuss the apologists on the fate of the lost → removed from the Held-by line, its statements reported in the footnote (source: vatican.va ITC, Some Current Questions in Eschatology §§4.2, 4.5)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality.html (Arnobius entry), arnobius-of-sicca.html and arnobius-patristic-witness.html: Moule's statement that Gelasius banished Arnobius's treatise was repeated without noting that the decree's attribution is disputed → added McCracken's description of it as a "sixth-century" decree "once ascribed to Pope Gelasius I" and von Dobschütz's dating (519-553, marked unverified) (source: McCracken, ACW 7, 1949, 33-34; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gelasian_Decree)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality.html (confessional bodies): the claim that Seventh-day Adventism drew on Storrs's Six Sermons (1842) had no footnote → footnoted to Burt, who dates the Six Sermons' first publication to Albany, 1842 (source: Merlin D. Burt, "Millerite Conditionalism: A Doctrine Adopted by Seventh-day Adventists", Adventist Archives Lecture, 2024, pp. 2-3, 11, documents.adventistarchives.org)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/acts-of-paul-and-thecla.html: Tertullian quoted as saying the presbyter wrote the Acts "out of love for Paul" → "confessing that he had done it from love of Paul" (source: Tertullian, On Baptism 17, ANF, Writings-Database/Tertullian/On Baptism.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/abercius.html: the inscription was called "the oldest dated monument to name the Eucharist" and Alexander's epitaph said to "quote" it, citing Wikipedia, which says neither → the Eucharistic reading is attributed to Henri Leclercq (Catholic Encyclopedia, 1913); Alexander's epitaph corresponds "almost word for word" and Abercius's is dated before 216 on linguistic and palaeographic grounds (Blank 2023) (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inscription_of_Abercius; en.wikisource.org Catholic Encyclopedia (1913)/Inscription of Abercius)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/c-s-lewis.html: the "I believe in Purgatory … Romish doctrine" quotation and the More/Fisher/Gerontius/"At our age" material were cited to churchpop.com, which does not contain them → added a source that reproduces the Letter 20 passage; churchpop kept for the quotations it does carry (source: thulcandra.wordpress.com/2007/11/17/c-s-lewis-on-purgatory/; churchpop.com/why-the-protestant-c-s-lewis-believed-in-purgatory/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/caesarius-of-arles.html: the quotation "cannot be purged in that transitory fire of which the apostle spoke, but the eternal flames will torture him without any remedy" was unsourced (it is not in the cited 1 Cor 3:15 verse entry) → cited to Sermon 179.2 as quoted by Catholic Answers, which also gives the date 522 (source: catholic.com/tract/mortal-sin)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/gregory-of-nyssa.html: Great Catechism quoted as restoring "the author of evil himself" → "healing even the introducer of evil himself" (ch. 26); the claim that Gregory places the purgation after the resurrection → replaced by the NPNF translator's note, which says the Judgment comes "after the Resurrection … and after the purifying and chastising" (source: Writings-Database/Gregory of Nyssa/The Great Catechism.html and On the Soul and the Resurrection.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/ambrose-of-milan.html: "Masses of Requiem" footnoted as Catholic Encyclopedia (1913), unattributed → Pietro Piacenza, Catholic Encyclopedia vol. 12 (1911) (source: newadvent.org/cathen/12776d.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arguments/ante-nicene-conditionalism.html: the "too inconsistent" reading, whose named holder is Fudge, said Tatian's "death by punishment in immortality" "can be taken either way", but Fudge (as Farrar reports him) counts Tatian among the first unambiguous holders of unending conscious punishment → Tatian clause dropped from that reading; fn 17 reports Fudge's view of Tatian and files Bobichon on Justin's coherence only, grouped by Farrar with J. C. M. van Winden under "the incoherence solution" (source: blog.dianoigo.com/2019/02/review-of-fire-that-consumes-by-edward.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality.html: Fudge said to treat the second-century statements, Tatian's included, as inconclusive, and Tatian's phrase said to have "been read both ways" (unattributed) → Fudge's "inconclusive" narrowed to Justin; on Tatian, Roller ("Tatian, like his famous tutor, was a Conditionalist", pp. 47-48) and Fudge as Farrar reports him (first unambiguous holders of unending punishment) are named (source: Roller 2008 PDF at 3ringbinder.org, p. 48; blog.dianoigo.com review of Fudge, part 3)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arguments/arnobius-patristic-witness.html: fn 2 cited Moule and Shahan as critics agreeing that Arnobius taught annihilation, but neither says so (they say the soul depends on God's gift for its duration) → critics' agreement cited to Salmond 473, Hontheim ("later on also Arnobius" among conditionalists) and McCracken, ACW 7, p. 20; Moule and Shahan kept for the gift-of-immortality point (source: archive.org Salmond 5th ed. djvu text p. 473; newadvent.org/cathen/07207a.htm; archive.org ancientchristian009983mbp djvu text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/conditional-immortality/arnobius-of-sicca.html: fn 7 supported "Readers on both sides agree" (annihilation) with Moule and Shahan, who do not say it → Salmond 473, Hontheim and McCracken p. 20 added as the critics who agree; Moule and Shahan cited for his account of the soul (source: as for arnobius-patristic-witness.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/tertullian.html: summary called Tertullian "the earliest Latin witness" to offerings for the dead, contradicting the same timeline's Passion of Perpetua (Carthage, c. 203, before Tertullian's c. 211) → "a Latin witness" (source: the timeline's own dates on perpetua.html and tertullian.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/martin-luther.html: summary linked the words "Apology of the Augsburg Confession" to the Formula of Concord page (a different document) → link removed, Apology named as Melanchthon's 1531 text later included in the Book of Concord (source: thebookofconcord.org Apology art. XXIV, opened)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html (consecration entry): Socrates VII.29 quoted as "although his discourse was extremely gratifying ... antipathy to even the name of heretic" → "although these utterances were extremely gratifying ... antipathy to the very name of heretic" (source: ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf202.ii.x.xxix.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html (Theotokos-sermons entry + footnote 2): quotations "from mere boyish folly" and "drawn upon himself… the reputation among the ignorant" attributed to Socrates VII.32 appear neither there nor in Evagrius I.2 → replaced with Socrates's verbatim words ("found him an unlearned man"; "I cannot then concede that he was either a follower of Paul of Samosata or of Photinus…"; "Nestorius thus acquired the reputation among the masses…") (sources: ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf202.ii.x.xxxii.html; tertullian.org/fathers/evagrius_1_book1.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html and cyril-nestorius/nestorius.html: Socrates called "no friend to him" on the timeline and "often sympathetic to him" on the person page (self-contradiction; Socrates says he writes "in entire freedom from personal antipathies") → both characterizations dropped, Socrates's own words quoted (source: ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf202.ii.x.xxxii.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html and cyril-nestorius/nestorius.html: the Bazaar of Heracleides said to be "lost for fifteen centuries" and Bethune-Baker listed among scholars responding to its 1910 publication → the single Syriac manuscript survived in the library of the Church of the East's patriarch at Kotchanes, was copied from 1889, used unpublished by Bethune-Baker (1908), and published by Bedjan (1910) (sources: tertullian.org/fathers/nestorius_bazaar_0_intro.htm; Bethune-Baker, Nestorius and His Teaching, preface, archive.org/details/nestoriushisteac00bethrich)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html and cyril-nestorius/nestorius.html: J. A. McGuckin listed with Bethune-Baker and Loofs among scholars who "reopened the question whether Nestorius was ever really a 'Nestorian'" → reported separately as holding that mid-twentieth-century accounts "romanticise" Nestorius (marked ⚠ unverified; his book not opened) (source: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ephesus)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius/cyril-of-alexandria.html (+ timeline footnote 13): Letter 96 described as "to Paul the Prefect" and "trans. R. Pearse" → a catalogue of gifts to many recipients (Paul the Prefect listed first), in an English translation Pearse reproduces and takes to be the Fathers of the Church version (source: roger-pearse.com/weblog/2010/08/20/cyril-of-alexandria-buys-the-government/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html (Ephesus-opens entry): "quoted back at him by Nestorius, whose colleague Memnon added" read Memnon as Nestorius's colleague → Memnon identified as bishop of Ephesus, quoted in the acts with Cyril (sources: Bazaar of Heracleides Book 2 part 1, tertullian.org/fathers/nestorius_bazaar_4_book2_part1.htm; newadvent.org/fathers/3810.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/tertullian.html: the "uttermost farthing" / "compensatory discipline" passage was cited as De Anima 35 (citation line and Reception list) → De Anima 58 (source: Writings-Database/Tertullian/A Treatise on the Soul.html, "Chapter LVIII.-Conclusion")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius/nestorius.html: the passage "Christ in that [he is] Christ is not divided; for we have not two Christs or two sons…" was presented as from the Bazaar of Heracleides (c. 450), but it is Fragm. 292 in the "Homilies" section of Driver & Hodgson's Appendix I, translated from Loofs, Nestoriana (1905) → re-attributed as a homily fragment and moved to the 428–430 section; the Bazaar section now quotes Bazaar Book 2, part 1, p. 153 ("I seek not to make as it were two sons…"); footnotes 2 and 7 corrected (source: Writings-Database Nestorius/The Bazaar of Heracleides/Appendices - fragments.html and Book 2, part 1.html, trans. Driver & Hodgson 1925)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html, cyril-nestorius/cyril-of-alexandria.html, cyril-nestorius/nestorius.html: Cyril's gift catalogue (Letter 96) was dated to the months after June 431 and set beside Theodosius's 431 change of course → dated to the 432–433 reunion negotiations (sent "at the same time" as Epiphanius's letter to Maximian), with the 431 bribery charge reported separately as the Antiochenes' allegation (Acacius of Beroea; Nestorius on Count John) (source: F. Loofs, Nestorius and His Place in the History of Christian Doctrine, 1914, Lecture II, en.wikisource.org; C. J. Hefele, History of the Councils, vol. 3, trans. 1883, §§150, 152, 156, archive.org/details/historyofcouncil03hefeuoft)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius/cyril-of-alexandria.html: Dates line gave "d. 27 June 444" while the body marked the day ⚠ unverified and footnote 7 says the cited article gives only the year (27 June being a feast day) → "d. 444" (source: the page's own footnote 7, citing Wikipedia "Cyril of Alexandria")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html: the blockquoted deposition decree opened with an ellipsis that cut its stated ground → restored "Compelled thereto by the canons and by the letter… of our most holy father and fellow-servant Cœlestine, the Roman bishop," (Greek parenthetical elided) (source: Decree of the Council against Nestorius, NPNF² vol. 14, newadvent.org/fathers/3810.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/eternal-conscious-torment/arguments/ante-nicene-dominance.html: McClymond was reported as stating that named second-century witnesses (1 Clement, Ignatius, Justin, Theophilus, Irenaeus) "agreed in teaching everlasting punishment"; his sentence is conditional ("To the extent that second-century authors agreed in teaching everlasting punishment, the claim of an unbroken tradition of Christian universalism is called into question") and names no such list → quoted in full; his actual list (p. 822) given separately (source: McClymond, Theological Studies 76.4 (2015), pp. 822-823, https://theologicalstudies.net/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/7-McClymond-Origenes-Vindicatus.pdf)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/aerius.html (body + footnote) and purgatory.html (footnotes aerius-1, arg-prayer-uncontested): "Augustine merely epitomises Epiphanius" credited to Henry Wace, the editor → the article "Aerius, founder of the heretical sect of the Aerians," signed E.V., in Wace and Piercy (eds.), Dictionary of Christian Biography (1911), matching the prayer-for-the-dead-uncontested page (source: ccel.org/ccel/wace/biodict.html?term=Aerius,+founder+of+the+heretical+sect+of+the+Aerians, signature "[E.V.]")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/purgatory/gregory-of-nyssa.html: Lactantius listed among Latin witnesses to "a post-mortem purifying fire," contradicting his own entry, which sets the fire at the Last Judgment → Lactantius (and Cyprian, whose Ep. 55.20 reading is disputed on his page) moved to "compare" (source: the topic's own Lactantius page, Divine Institutes VII.21, Writings-Database/Lucius Caecilius Firmianus Lactantius/The Divine Institutes Book 7.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html: Loofs quoted selectively, as calling Cyril's assembly of 22 June 431 alone a "party-council", while Loofs applies the label to both assemblies ("one party-council standing in opposition to the other") → one-sided sentence removed from the 22 June entry; Loofs's symmetric wording quoted in the 26–27 June entry (source: F. Loofs, Nestorius and His Place in the History of Christian Doctrine, 1914, Lecture II, en.wikisource.org)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/cyril-nestorius.html and cyril-nestorius/cyril-of-alexandria.html: Cyril described as venerated in the Catholic and Eastern Orthodox churches but only "kept in the Coptic Orthodox calendar", understating the cited source → venerated in the Catholic, Eastern Orthodox and Oriental Orthodox churches and in Anglican and Lutheran calendars, with feast days (source: Cyril of Alexandria, Wikipedia, infobox "Venerated in" and "Feast")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/eternal-conscious-torment/basil-of-caesarea.html: Ramelli reported (⚠ unverified, from her book) as noting that Shorter Rules 267 would condemn "Basil's own teacher Origen" and his siblings; Origen (d. c. 254) was not Basil's teacher, and Ramelli's own wording names his brother and sister → replaced with Ramelli's own words ("his own brother, whom he appointed bishop, and saintly sister are inspired by the devil") and her interpolation / pastoral-audience hypothesis, cited, ⚠ unverified marker removed (source: I. Ramelli, "Reply to Professor Michael McClymond," Theological Studies 76.4 (2015), 831-832, https://theologicalstudies.net/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/8-Ramelli-Reply-to-Professor.pdf)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/eternal-conscious-torment.html: Hippolytus entry said *Against Plato, on the Cause of the Universe* was "transmitted under his name", contradicting his detail page ("long transmitted under other names (including Josephus's)") → "printed among his fragments in the Ante-Nicene Fathers; its attribution is discussed on his detail page" (source: the page's own person page and its cited ANF vol. 5 fragments)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/eternal-conscious-torment.html: Ramelli's view on Basil, *Shorter Rules* 267, narrated as "holds … probably an interpolation", firmer than her own words quoted in the footnote ("Here I hypothesize … an interpolation") → "hypothesizes that … is an interpolation" (source: the page's own cited Ramelli quotation)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/eternal-conscious-torment/john-chrysostom.html: "loss of God" claim was ⚠ unverified and mislocated (not in Homily 3 on 2 Thessalonians) → located and quoted verbatim from Homilies on Matthew, Homily 23, §9 ("I affirm the loss of that glory to be a far greater punishment than hell"), marker removed (source: https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/200123.htm, NPNF1 vol. 10)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: said Trent's canons "condemn the Protestant reading by name" → canons anathematize "any one" who holds the stated propositions; none names Luther or a reformer (source: Council of Trent, Session VI, https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/trent/sixth-session.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: attributed "truly merit" (Canon 32's wording) to Chapter XVI → Chapter XVI's own phrase "to have truly merited eternal life" (source: Council of Trent, Session VI, https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/trent/sixth-session.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/indulgences.html: applied Thomas Madden's "the seed that in three centuries' time would grow into the weed of abuses" to Urban II's Clermont grant (1095); Madden says it of the provision of Quia maior / Lateran IV (1213–1215) letting those who paid to outfit a crusader share the indulgence → sentence re-anchored to that provision, footnote now cites the work and chapter (source: Madden, The New Concise History of the Crusades, ch. 8, erenow.org).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/indulgences.html + indulgences/johann-tetzel.html: Fugger loan and half-and-half division of the St. Peter's proceeds cited to Catholic Encyclopedia "Albert of Brandenburg"/"Johann Tetzel" and Britannica "Johann Tetzel", none of which (as opened) states it → claim kept, marked ⚠ unverified, footnote notes the gap (sources opened: newadvent.org/cathen/01262a.htm, 14539a.htm; archived britannica.com/biography/Johann-Tetzel).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/indulgences/arguments/reform-concedes-critique.html: "The claim" blockquote was an unattributed composed statement presented as a quotation → replaced with verbatim Trent Session XXV decree and Indulgentiarum Doctrina Norm 4 (sources: papalencyclicals.net Trent Session 25; vatican.va Indulgentiarum Doctrina).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: Trent Session VI can. 9 quoted only as "that by faith alone the impious is justified", dropping its qualifying clause (also paraphrased in the positions-in-brief as a blanket anathema) → quoted through "…by the movement of his own will" and the brief paraphrase now quotes the "in such wise as to mean…" clause (source: papalencyclicals.net/councils/trent/sixth-session.htm, Canon IX)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: Augustine, De gratia et libero arbitrio 15, "It is His own gifts that God crowns, not your merits." ended with a full stop, dropping the condition "—if, at least, your merits are of your own self, not of Him…" (text and footnote) → quotation extended; De spiritu et littera 15 "justified freely by His grace" extended with "—not that it is wrought without our will; … that grace may heal its infirmity" (source: newadvent.org/fathers/1510.htm, /1502.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: positions-in-brief said the Lutheran and Reformed confessions teach justification "through faith alone", footnoted to Augsburg IV and Westminster XI.1, neither of which says "alone" → "freely through faith", adding Westminster XI.2's "the alone instrument of justification" and citing §§1–2 (source: the-highway.com/WCFChXI.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: Catholic Response (1998) paraphrase dropped its qualifier, making a difficulty with the Joint Declaration's presentation read as a judgement on the Lutheran simul iustus et peccator itself → restored "in the current state of the presentation, given in the Joint Declaration" (source: christianunity.va Response, en1.html, Clarification 1)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/justification.html: Trent Session VI ch. XVI quoted as "to have truly merited eternal life", cut before its conditions (text and footnote) → quoted "by those very works which have been done in God… to have truly merited eternal life, to be obtained also in its (due) time, if so be, however, that they depart in grace" plus "God forbid that a Christian should either trust or glory in himself…" (source: papalencyclicals.net Trent sixth session)

- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/arguments/tertullian-helvidian.html: Jerome's reply to Helvidius cited as Against Helvidius §17 with a link to the NPNF translation, where the passage stands in §19, and an English rendering ('he was not a man of the Church') attributed to Lightfoot's translation, though Lightfoot quotes only the Latin and paraphrases 'not a member of the Church' → passage now quoted in Fremantle's NPNF wording ('Of Tertullian I say no more than that he did not belong to the Church', §19), with the Latin and Lightfoot's '§17' citation kept in the note (source: Jerome, The Perpetual Virginity of Blessed Mary §19, NPNF 2nd ser. vol. 6, local Writings-Database copy; Lightfoot, 'The Brethren of the Lord' §8, biblestudytools.com)

- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/synod-of-jerusalem.html: footnote 1 attributed the 16 March 1672 date, 'signed by Dositheus and some sixty-eight', and the 'most important council… comparable to Trent' ranking to Wikipedia, which gives 20 March 1672 for the acts, 'sixty-eight signatures in total', and quotes the ranking from Schaff → footnote restates Wikipedia accurately; the 16 March date is credited to Schaff ('dated Jerusalem, March 16, 1672') and both dates are reported in the Dates line and Provenance (source: Wikipedia 'Synod of Jerusalem (1672)'; Schaff, Creeds of Christendom vol. 1 §17, ccel.org/ccel/schaff/creeds1.v.vii.html)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/vincent-of-lerins.html: the Latin behind 'the divine Oracles cry aloud' (Commonitory 21) given as 'divina eloquia' → 'cum divina clament oracula', with the Latin text cited (source: Vicentius Lerinensis, Commonitorium ch. 21, thelatinlibrary.com/vicentius.html)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/tertullian.html: 'Tertullian's Latin reckons it "Dan. xiii."' (Susanna, De Corona 4) → the 'Dan. xiii' reference is the ANF editors' note ('Vulgate, Dan. xiii. 32. [See Apocrypha, Hist. of Susanna, v. 32.]'), not Tertullian's text (source: Tertullian, The Chaplet ch. 4, ANF vol. 3, local Writings-Database copy, n. 12)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/synopsis-scripturae-sacrae.html: summary line called §74 'a later appendix' while the body calls it 'a later section of the work' → 'a later section of the work (§74)'; the sixth-century dating ('most scholarship') now attributed to the translator's note it rests on, quoted (source: bible-researcher.com/sss.html)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/westminster-confession.html: footnote 2 spoke of 'the eight books in view' but named seven books plus the Greek additions, and WCF 1.3 enumerates none → states that 1.3 does not enumerate the books and lists the named books without a count (source: the page's own quotation of WCF 1.3, apuritansmind.com/westminster-standards/chapter-1/)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/ambrose-of-milan.html: body and footnote 2 said Ambrose stands 'last' in Lightfoot's column B; the conspectus continues '…Ambrose; [Chrysostom]; Cyril of Alex.…' → placed 'immediately after' Gregory of Nyssa and Epiphanius, conspectus quoted further (source: Lightfoot, 'The Brethren of the Lord', conspectus, https://web.archive.org/web/20180618231826/https://philologos.org/__eb-jbl/brethren.htm)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/ambrose-of-milan.html: body quoted the treatise as saying one who pursues the question 'will find' it; the cited translation reads 'they would find it' → 'would find' (source: Ambrose, De Institutione Virginis ch. VI, local Writings-Database English translation)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/ambrose-of-milan.html: note said the Capua synod (391) referred Bonosus's case 'to the bishops of Macedonia and to Ambrose'; the letter on the case records referral to the Macedonian bishops under the bishop of Thessalonica, with Bonosus afterwards consulting Ambrose → restated and cited; date left to sources (Lightfoot 392, Catholic Encyclopedia 391) (source: 'Letter on the case of Bonosus', Letters of S. Ambrose, Library of Fathers 1881, pp. 339-340, local Writings-Database copy)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/bonosus-and-jovinian.html: unsourced 'the see of Rome under Siricius held him a heretic on the point' and 'condemned by the synod of Capua' stated as settled → replaced by the letter's own words ('justly blamed concerning the sons of Mary'), Capua's referral to the Macedonian bishops, and Lightfoot's 'condemned by a synod assembled at Capua' reported as his (source: 'Letter on the case of Bonosus' §§1, 3 and editor's preface, as above; Lightfoot §22)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/clement-of-alexandria.html: summary/notes/footnote 2 said Lightfoot placed Clement 'second, after the Gospel of Peter and Protevangelium' (i.e. third) → 'after the Gospel of Peter and Protevangelium' (source: Lightfoot conspectus, column B, https://web.archive.org/web/20180618231826/https://philologos.org/__eb-jbl/brethren.htm)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/cyril-of-jerusalem.html: said that 'alone among that group' Cyril is marked with a query; Lightfoot's list also reads 'Clementine Homilies (?)' → both queried entries reported (source: Lightfoot conspectus, 'A. or B.', https://web.archive.org/web/20180618231826/https://philologos.org/__eb-jbl/brethren.htm)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/clementine-literature.html: stated that 'these Ebionite circles denied the virgin birth outright' / Ebionites 'denied the virgin birth'; Eusebius reports two Ebionite groups, one of which 'did not deny that the Lord was born of a virgin' → Eusebius's report given and cited (source: Eusebius, Church History iii.27.1-3, NPNF trans., local Writings-Database copy)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/augustine-of-hippo.html: note said the Clopas=Alphaeus identification 'enters the tradition only later, with Chrysostom and Theodoret' as fact → reported as Lightfoot's 'It occurs first, I believe, in Chrysostom… and after him in Theodoret', quoted in the footnote (source: Lightfoot §3, https://web.archive.org/web/20180618231826/https://philologos.org/__eb-jbl/brethren.htm)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/athanasius-of-alexandria.html: footnote 2 was never cited in the text → cited on the Date note; its 'the best judges assign them' restated as Robertson's own quoted words (source: Robertson, introduction to the Four Discourses, NPNF 2/4, biblestudytools.com)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/synod-of-jerusalem.html and ot-canon.html (Synod of Jerusalem entry): said the catechism of Metropolitan Philaret "distinguishes" the disputed books as "anaginoskomena", "ranked below the protocanonical books" (sourced only to Wikipedia) → reported from the catechism itself: it counts twenty-two books with Cyril, Athanasius and John of Damascus, omits Sirach "and certain others" "Because they do not exist in the Hebrew", and, after Athanasius, says they "have been appointed of the Fathers to be read by proselytes"; it does not use the term (source: Longer Catechism Q31–35, Blackmore trans. in Schaff, https://www.pravoslavieto.com/docs/eng/Orthodox_Catechism_of_Philaret.htm)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/arguments/apostolic-bible-was-the-septuagint.html: internal cross-references pointed at the wrong notes ("see Archer & Chirichigno, n. 3", Archer being n. 2; "Hengel and Lim (n. 2)", Hengel/Lim being the Sundberg–Hengel–Lim note) → n. 2 and n. 12 (source: the page's own Sources list)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/arguments/non-canonical-authority-judgment.html: footnote 3 assigned the inference to "the shorter-canon side's (n. 1)", but that side's note is n. 2 → n. 2 (source: the page's own Sources list)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/arguments/trent-first-universal-binding-definition.html: Catholic Answers was credited with placing conciliar infallibility "in the binding ecumenical definition" and with the phrase "not infallible in the highest sense until Trent", which neither cited Catholic Answers article contains → the phrase is given to Dave Armstrong only (his words: "the canonical lists were not infallible in the highest sense until Trent", crediting Patti Sheffield); Catholic Answers quoted in its own words: Trent "infallibly reiterated what the Church had long taught", "the first infallible conciliar listing of each individual book" (source: Catholic Answers, "Did the Church add the Deuterocanonical books to the Bible at the Council of Trent?" and "Was the Council of Trent inconsistent…", catholic.com, opened; Armstrong, "1 Esdras & the Canon of Hippo, Carthage, & Trent", Patheos 2020, web.archive.org copy, opened)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/arguments/canon-councils-contradict.html: same misattribution of "not infallible in the highest sense until Trent" and of an infallibility-in-ecumenical-definition thesis to Catholic Answers (body and n. 9) → corrected as above (same sources)
- 2026-11-24 ot-canon/council-of-trent.html: body and n. 6 said the Orthodox keeping of 1 Esdras and the Prayer of Manasseh was "affirmed at the Synod of Jerusalem (1672)"; the Confession of Dositheus, Decree 3 (quoted on the topic's synod-of-jerusalem.html), names Wisdom, Judith, Tobit, Bel, Susanna, the Maccabees and Sirach, not 1 Esdras or the Prayer of Manasseh → Synod attribution dropped; n. 6 now quotes Ware's list of the Orthodox Deutero-Canonical Books (opening "3 Esdras") and the cited Wikipedia article on the prayer (source: synod-of-jerusalem.html, Decree 3; Ware, The Orthodox Church, Part II, fatheralexander.org excerpt, opened; Wikipedia "Prayer of Manasseh", opened)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/epiphanius-of-salamis.html (fn 6): quotation of Lightfoot ended "would have hailed with delight… had he known of it", words not in Lightfoot → replaced with Lightfoot's actual wording "would have hailed with delight a solution which, as Jerome boasted, saved the virginity not of Mary only but of Joseph also" (source: Lightfoot, "The Brethren of the Lord", §21, philologos.org via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/didymus-the-blind.html: page dated the De Trinitate passage c. 386 yet said Didymus wrote "before" Jerome's reply to Helvidius (c. 383) → reworded to "in the same decade as" (source: the page's own dating, c. 381–392)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/j-b-lightfoot.html: body said Luther, Calvin and Zwingli "defend the virginity without saying who the brethren were", contradicting the page's own note that Luther (outright) and Calvin (more cautiously) favoured the Hieronymian cousin-reading → replaced with "Lightfoot states which of the three solutions he prefers" (source: the page's own note and the Luther/Calvin detail pages)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/jerome.html: Lightfoot quoted as saying Epiphanius "would have hailed with delight… had he known of it" (words not in Lightfoot) → quotation trimmed to Lightfoot's actual words "would have hailed with delight" (source: Lightfoot, "The Brethren of the Lord", §21, philologos.org via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/jerome.html: Lightfoot quoted as concluding the Hieronymian theory must "lead to its rejection", dropping his condition → restored "must lead to its rejection, if any hypothesis less burdened with difficulties can be found" in body and footnote 8 (source: Lightfoot, "The Brethren of the Lord", philologos.org via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 perpetual-virginity/jerome.html: Lightfoot's evidence for four women at John 19:25 listed as "Peshito, the Jerusalem Syriac lectionary, the Thebaic"; Lightfoot cites the Peshito and the Jerusalem Syriac lectionary only → "the Thebaic" removed (source: Lightfoot, "The Brethren of the Lord", philologos.org via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/first-condemnation-disciplinary.html: Eusebius, Church History VI.23.4 was quoted as “was agitated concerning him”, which is not the wording of the text → quoted verbatim as “the matters that were agitated concerning him on this account” (source: Writings-Database, Eusebius of Caesarea/THE CHURCH HISTORY OF EUSEBIUS/Book 6.html, VI.23.4)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/leo-the-great.html: Chalcedon acclamation quoted as “Peter has spoken through Leo”, unsourced → “Peter has spoken thus through Leo”, now footnoted (source: Wikipedia, “Pope Leo I”, quoting the conciliar acts)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/tertullian.html: summary called a virginity “outlasting the birth” the virginitas in partu, and rendered Jerome’s ecclesiae hominem as “not a member of the Church” against the page’s own “not a man of the Church” → in partu described as virginity through the birth; translation harmonized (source: Jerome, Against Helvidius §17, as cited on the page)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/theodoret-of-cyrus.html: called Theophylact “the timeline’s one later witness”, though John of Damascus, Aquinas and others are later on the same timeline → removed (source: the perpetual-virginity timeline’s own entries)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/theophylact-of-ohrid.html: said Lightfoot filed the levirate theory “among the shifts he judged untenable”; Lightfoot applies “untenable” to Gregory of Nyssa’s identification, and of Theophylact says only that the suggestion “found but little favour” → removed (source: Lightfoot, “The Brethren of the Lord”, philologos.org archive copy)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/martin-luther.html: said Calvin dismissed the inference from “firstborn” and “brethren”; Calvin’s cited texts address “firstborn” and “till” (Matt 1:25) → corrected; also “settles on” the cousin sense → “inclines to”, matching Luther’s “I am inclined to agree” (source: john-calvin.html Harmony quotations; Luther, Sermons on John, LW 22, as quoted on the page)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/john-wesley.html: called Wesley the latest Protestant to profess the doctrine, though J. B. Lightfoot (1865, Epiphanian) is later on the same timeline → removed (source: j-b-lightfoot.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/thomas-aquinas.html: interval from Theophylact given as “two centuries” in the summary and “a century and a half” in the notes → “two centuries” in both (c. 1075 vs c. 1270, the page dates)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/not-condemned-by-ecumenical-council.html: Richard Price quoted as saying the 543 anathemas' authority was "imperial rather than synodal" (cited to the afkimel 543-canons page); the phrase is not on that page or in Price's Appendix I → replaced with Price's own wording, "Justinian issued an edict and a set of canons condemning Origenism" (source: Price, Acts of the Council of Constantinople of 553, vol. 2, p. 273, https://afkimel.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/the-acts-of-the-council-of-constantinople-of-553-2-vol-edited-and-translated-by-richard-price-translated-texts-for-historians-volume-51-2009-9781846311789-da31e1d5d5690161fc03aafa9daef89.pdf)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/not-condemned-by-ecumenical-council.html: Franz Diekamp presented as "the scholarly spine" of the view that the council did not anathematize Origen; Diekamp dates the fifteen to the bishops' meeting before the opening but rejects "mit aller Bestimmtheit" the forgery of Origen's name in the eleventh canon and holds Origen anathematized at the synod → listed under the reading that the council's bishops condemned Origen (source: Diekamp, Die origenistischen Streitigkeiten, 1899, pp. 73-75, https://archive.org/details/dieorigenistisch00diek)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/metempsychosis-charge.html: Commentary on Matthew XIII.2 quoted as “not the soul… but the spirit and the power” and “he did not say in the ‘soul’ of Elijah” → “Elijah, therefore, was not called John because of the soul, but because of the spirit and the power” and “he did not say in the soul of Elijah” (source: ANF IX, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/101613.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/second-crucifixion-for-demons.html: Justinian's seventh anathema was quoted in Richard Price's wording but cited only to NPNF2 XIV, whose wording differs → footnote now credits Price's translation (Eclectic Orthodoxy reproduction) and gives the NPNF wording (source: https://afkimel.wordpress.com/2015/01/27/the-canons-of-the-synod-of-constantinople-543/ ; https://www.tertullian.org/fathers2/NPNF2-14/Npnf2-14-117.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/second-crucifixion-for-demons.html: Theophilus's festal letter of 401 (Jerome, Letter 96) was cited as NPNF2 VI, but the English at the cited link is a machine translation of Jerome's Latin → footnote now says so (source: https://historicalchristian.faith/by_father.php?file=Jerome%2FLetters%2Fletter_096.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/second-crucifixion-for-demons.html: "crucified for them as well" was given as Jerome's admitted corollary; the words are Theophilus's (festal letter of 401, §10) → reattributed to Theophilus (source: Jerome, Letter 96, same link)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/second-crucifixion-for-demons.html: De Principiis III.6.5 ("mind and hostile will") was presented as Origen's account of reconciling the fallen orders; the passage concerns "the last enemy", death → context stated as Origen's (source: ANF IV, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/04123.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/victorinus-of-pettau.html: said Jerome "quotes" the words of Victorinus without reproducing them; in the passage it is Helvidius who "quotes the words of Victorinus" → reworded to "Jerome reports that Helvidius quotes the words of Victorinus but does not reproduce them himself" (source: Jerome, Against Helvidius 19, NPNF2 vol. 6, Writings-Database/Jerome/The Perpetual Virginity of Blessed Mary.html)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/resurrection-of-the-flesh.html: Augustine quoted inline as the flesh not "changed into spirit, as some suppose who misinterpret the text"; the text reads "Not that it is converted into spirit, as some fancy from the words" → inline misquote removed, correct wording kept in the footnote (source: City of God XIII.20, NPNF1 II, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/120113.htm)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/resurrection-of-the-flesh.html: Justinian's edict of 543, anathema 5, quoted as "human bodies will rise spherical in form" (wording not in the cited Price translation) → "at the resurrection the bodies of human beings will be raised spherical" (source: canons of 543, tr.
