Ambrose (Oxford, 1881), Writings-Database/Ambrose of Milan/Letters/Letters 51-60.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/arguments/late-definition-charge.html: footnote quoted James White's list as "Have you thought through the entire complex of doctrines and dogmas relating to Mary, and how unbiblical and a-historical they are? … Are you aware of the origin of these doctrines…", wording not on the cited page → replaced with the list's text, "Have you considered how completely unbiblical and a-historical is the entire complex of doctrines and dogmas related to Mary? … Are you aware that such doctrines as perpetual virginity and bodily assumption have their origin in gnosticism…" (source: https://www.aomin.org/aoblog/roman-catholicism/reposting-a-top-ten-list-on-roman-catholicism/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/arguments/clement-textual-reliability.html (also clement-of-alexandria.html and the timeline footnote): the caution "We cannot rely absolutely on this text…" was attributed to Juniper B. Carol; it is Philip J. Donnelly, S.J., "The Perpetual Virginity of the Mother of God", in Carol (ed.), Mariology vol. 2 (1957), p. 271 n. 149, a note on the Latin Adumbrationes, followed by "there is no indication that in the matter of Mary's virginity there was any emendation of Clement's own words" → attributed to Donnelly in Carol's volume (source: https://archive.org/details/mariologyvol2, full text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/arguments/clement-textual-reliability.html: Thorncrown quotation began "Clement of Alexandria initially appears…"; the source reads "Clement of Alexandria, initially appears…" → comma restored (source: https://thorncrownministries.com/blog/2017/07/07/roman-catholics-and-their-queen-part-4)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/arguments/late-definition-charge.html: Richard Bauckham was listed on the Held-by line of the reading that the belief "grew after the apostles", which he does not hold → removed from Held-by; his survey of exegetes' view of the brothers kept in the case with the footnote qualifier that he does not hold the reading (source: Bauckham, "The Relatives of Jesus", Themelios 21.2, already cited on the page: "The historical evidence is not sufficient for a firm decision between the Helvidian and Epiphanian views").
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/prokathartheisa.html: the passage blockquote inserted a Greek gloss ("(prokathartheisēs)") and replaced Gregory's parenthesis "(for it was needful…)" with a dash → restored the Browne & Swallow text verbatim, gloss moved outside the quotation (source: Gregory of Nazianzus, Oration 38 §13, NPNF 2.7, historicalchristian.faith/Writings-Database).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/prokathartheisa.html: the page said Kappes presses the prefix pro- back "to her origin"; Kappes reads prokathartheisa as a positive preparation in grace at the Annunciation, and grounds Mary's immaculacy from her first moment on the wider Greek tradition → restated (sources: Kimel, Eclectic Orthodoxy, 4 Dec 2019, quoting Kappes p. 26; Cuff, review of Kappes, Hortulus 13.1, 2017).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/prokathartheisa.html + all-holy-vocabulary.html: Kappes's quotation "never vacillated about the all-immaculate status of the Blessed Virgin Mary" was cut off at "Mary." with no ellipsis; the sentence goes on "from the first moment of her existence until her glorious assumption into heaven" (pp. 196-197) → quoted in full (source: Kimel, Eclectic Orthodoxy, 4 Dec 2019).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/all-holy-vocabulary.html: the Conception-feast hymn blockquote silently dropped the bracketed "[Mary]" present in the cited source → restored (source: Nishant Xavier, OnePeterFive, 8 Dec 2021).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/all-holy-vocabulary.html: Lev Gillet was cited only as conceding that "all holy / immaculate" titles do not imply the doctrine; his essay concludes the doctrine "has every right to its existence in the Orthodox Church as an opinion of a school or as a personal theologoumenon" and cites Byzantine homilies for it → filed under that reading with his concession attributed (source: Gillet, Chrysostom 6.5, 1983, reprinted on Eclectic Orthodoxy, 23 Sept 2021).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/aquinas-denial.html: Cole & Belanger (Nova et Vetera 4.3, 2006) were cited as the modern statement of the Thomist "vindication" case; they argue "that Aquinas's basic argument is against the idea of the Immaculate Conception" and call the did-not-deny view "an incorrect judgment" (pp. 474, 478) → filed under the denial reading, with their partial agreement ("close to the dogma", p. 483) attributed (source: the Nova et Vetera PDF at isidore.co).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/aquinas-denial.html: the Summa passage "did indeed contract original sin, but was cleansed therefrom before her birth from the womb" is in the reply to objection 2 of ST III q.27 a.2, not ad 3 → cited as ad 2 (source: newadvent.org/summa/4027.htm). The same "ad 3" label remains on the thomas-aquinas person page (not edited by this run).
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/john-calvin.html and docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity.html (Calvin entry): summary and timeline said Calvin's Matthean texts leave the identity of the brethren aside, omitting his Harmony comment on Matthew 13:55 ("Helvidius displayed excessive ignorance in concluding that Mary must have had many sons, because Christ's brothers are sometimes mentioned") → added a Matthew 13:55 section and adjusted the summary, reception note and timeline sentence (source: https://www.ccel.org/ccel/calvin/calcom32.ii.xxxix.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/john-calvin.html and docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity.html: the English of the 1562 sermon on Matthew 1:22-25 ("what folly this is!") was cited without translator or edition; its only located source is a 2009 reader's comment whose author is "not 100% sure where I got this citation from" → English rendering marked ⚠ unverified (source: https://beggarsallreformation.blogspot.com/2009/04/help-with-matthew-118-and-mary.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity.html (Zwingli entry): said Zwingli, "unlike Luther and Calvin," is not filed under any of the three readings, contradicting the Calvin entry and detail page, which do not file Calvin under any reading → reworded to "(as Luther and Calvin elsewhere do [take up the brethren]), so Zwingli is not filed" (source: the page's own Calvin entry and docs/doctrines/perpetual-virginity/john-calvin.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/stars-ensouled-rational-beings.html: Photius, Bibliotheca cod. 8, quoted as “the stars are alive” → “the stars being alive” (source: Photius, Bibliotheca cod. 8, tr. J. H. Freese, https://www.tertullian.org/fathers/photius_03bibliotheca.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/successive-worlds.html: the Stoic-recurrence quotations (“an arrangement of things in which everything will be unchanged…”, “Socrates will be again the son of Sophroniscus…”) were cited to Contra Celsum IV.67–68 → cited to V.20 (IV.67–68 kept as the parallel passage); “ever being brought into a better condition” cited to V.20 → V.21 (source: Origen, Contra Celsum Books 4–5, ANF IV, Writings-Database copy)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/ambrose-of-milan.html: the Notes cited the Catholic Encyclopedia's "never formally or explicitly" as a concession about the Latin witnesses including Ambrose, but the sentence concerns the Greek Fathers ("The Greek Fathers never formally or explicitly discussed the question of the Immaculate Conception") → misapplied quotation removed from the Ambrose page (source: Holweck, "Immaculate Conception," Catholic Encyclopedia, https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07674d.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/eastern-orthodox-rejection.html: Ware quoted as "in the past individual Orthodox have made statements which… approached close to it" → "In the past individual Orthodox have made statements which, if not definitely affirming the doctrine of the Immaculate Conception, at any rate approach close to it" (source: Ware, The Orthodox Church, https://www.fatheralexander.org/booklets/english/history_timothy_ware_2.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/francis-of-mayronis.html: said he was made master of theology in 1323 "at the request of Pope John XXII" → on 24 May 1323, at the request of King Robert of Naples, John XXII ordered the Paris chancellor to grant him the degree (source: "Francis Mayron," Catholic Encyclopedia, https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/10091b.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/council-of-trent.html: listed "the canon" (canon.html) among pages where the Council of Trent appears, but that page does not mention Trent → link pointed to the Old Testament canon page (ot-canon.html), which records Trent's canon decree (source: the two pages' own text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state.html: Gregory of Nyssa entry put "the bosom of Abraham … as a symbol of the good state of the soul" in quotation marks, but the cited text does not contain it → replaced with the verbatim "the name of Abraham's bosom is given to that good situation of the soul" (source: Gregory of Nyssa, On the Soul and the Resurrection, NPNF tr., local Writings-Database copy linked in the page's footnote 24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state.html: Hippolytus entry quoted "we call by the name Abraham's bosom" → "we call it by the name Abraham's bosom" (source: Hippolytus, Against Plato, on the Cause of the Universe, in Fragments — Dogmatical and Historical, ANF tr., local Writings-Database copy linked in footnote 15)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/text-transmission-partisan.html: quoted Rufinus's Peri Archon preface ("either omitted it... or set it down in terms agreeing with the rule of faith") citing newadvent Jerome Letter 80 labelled "NPNF² vol. 3"; that page is NPNF² vol. 6 and renders the sentence differently → quote now cited to NPNF² vol. 3 (tertullian.org Npnf2-03-35), Letter 80 kept and labelled vol. 6 (source: both pages opened)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/text-transmission-partisan.html: dated Rufinus's Latin Peri Archon "about 398" / "c. 398" → 397, the date of the preface (source: NPNF² vol. 3 heading, Preface to the Translations of Origen's Books Peri Archon, "Addressed to Macarius, at Pinetum, a.d. 397")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/text-transmission-partisan.html: listed Jerome's Letter 124 among the witnesses to the Greek of Peri Archon → described as preserving passages of Jerome's literal Latin version (source: Jerome, Letter 124 §1, NPNF² vol. 6, newadvent)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/papal-acts-1616-1708.html: said Ineffabilis Deus's "own text renews 'the Constitutions and Decrees issued by the Roman Pontiffs, our predecessors, especially Sixtus IV, Paul V, and Gregory XV'" → these are Alexander VII's words (Sollicitudo, 1661), quoted by Pius IX in Ineffabilis Deus (source: https://www.papalencyclicals.net/pius09/p9ineff.htm, section "Papal Sanctions")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/pope-sixtus-iv.html: Grave nimis blockquote elided "holding a contrary opinion, viz." and "incur the guilt of heresy, or deadly sin," so that it read as penalizing those who assert Mary was conceived with original sin → restored the full clause verbatim (source: http://catholicheritage.blogspot.com/2011/12/immaculate-conception-ii-grave-nimis.html; cf. Denzinger 735 at sensusfidelium.com)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/pope-pius-ix.html: quoted the Catholic Encyclopedia as calling kecharitomene "an illustration, not a proof of the dogma" → "serves only as an illustration, not as a proof of the dogma" (source: https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07674d.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/pope-pius-ix.html: put "all-holy" in quotation marks as Ineffabilis Deus's praise of Mary; the word is not in the bull's English → replaced with the bull's "the Virgin undefiled, immaculate, ever blessed, and free from all contagion of sin" (source: https://www.papalencyclicals.net/pius09/p9ineff.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/pope-pius-ix.html: dated Sixtus IV's feast constitution 1477, contradicting the Sixtus IV page → 1476 (source: Ineffabilis Deus n. 8, "Cum Praeexcelsa, February 28, 1476", papalencyclicals.net; Holweck, Catholic Encyclopedia, "28 February, 1476")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/jetzer-affair.html: said Rudolf Steck laid the blame "on Rome rather than Bern" → "laid responsibility on the pope" (source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jetzer_affair, "laid responsibility at the feet of the Pope")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/john-of-montson.html: called Holweck's "(John of Montesono, 1373)" an error and the 1387 date settled → both dates reported with their sources (O'Daniel, Catholic Encyclopedia; 1911 Britannica; Wikipedia: 1387; Holweck, Catholic Encyclopedia: 1373)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/epiphanius-of-salamis.html: said the Rufinus–Jerome exchange over the "six thousand books" took place "within twenty years of Epiphanius's death", though Jerome's reply dates from 402 and Epiphanius died in 403 → "in Epiphanius's own lifetime, Jerome's reply dating from 402" (source: the page's Dates line and its note 19, Jerome, Apology against Rufinus II.20–22, newadvent.org/fathers/27102.htm; the Origenist-controversy timeline, "Jerome's Apology against Rufinus, Books I–II (402)")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/augustine-of-hippo.html: said De haeresibus 43 gives the defence of Origen "the last word", though the Latin quoted on the page ends with the prosecution clause ("quamvis et in istis eum convincere studeant qui eius plura legerunt") → reworded to report both the defence and the note that fuller readers pressed the charges (source: the page's own quotation, la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_Haeresibus_ad_Quodvultdeum §43)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/second-council-of-constantinople.html: Notes called it "certain" that the fifteen anathemas are "of the 550s", contradicting the same page's report of Hefele assigning them to the synod of 543 → "of the 540s or 550s" (source: the page's own note 7, NPNF2 XIV Excursus on the XV anathemas quoting Hefele)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/excised-descent-prophecy.html: applied Justin's "is still written in some copies … (for it is only a short time since they were cut out)" to the descent saying, but in Dial. 72 the remark concerns the Jeremiah 11:19 saying quoted just before → reported as attached to Jer 11:19, with M. R. James's reading that it covers the descent saying too (source: Justin, Dialogue with Trypho 72, Writings-Database / https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/anf01.viii.iv.lxxii.html; James, Lost Apocrypha of the OT, 1920, p. 63, https://archive.org/details/lostapocryphaofo00jameuoft)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/excised-descent-prophecy.html: dated David A. Burnett's post 2017 and credited it with "the scribal-error suggestion" (Latin copyist writing Isaiah for Jeremiah) → dated 8 May 2009; the scribal-error attribution removed, as the post makes no such suggestion, and Burnett's own disclaimer ("I do not hold to this view") reported (source: https://www.dburnett.com/?p=124 via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/excised-descent-prophecy.html: quoted "the editors of the Ante-Nicene Fathers … at the point of citation" (AH III.20.4) as saying "wanting in our Scriptures … cited by Iren., iii. 20, under the name of Isaiah"; that wording is the note signed Maranus on Dial. 72 → attributed to that note; the AH III.20.4 note quoted in its own words (source: https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/anf01.viii.iv.lxxii.html; https://ccel.org/ccel/irenaeus/against_heresies_iii/anf01.ix.iv.xxi.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/excised-descent-prophecy.html: cited Swete for the claim that Justin is "nearly the only Greek writer to quote" the Ps 96:10 "from the wood" → Swete's statement reported: no existing Greek MS of the Psalter carries it except cod. 156, a ligno stands in Old Latin texts (source: Swete, Introduction to the OT in Greek, 1900, pp. 423-24, https://archive.org/details/introductiontool00swet)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/descent-clause.html: footnote quoted Formula of Concord SD IX as "we abide … by the simplicity of our Christian faith, whereof Dr. Luther has spoken in his sermon, delivered in the castle at Torgau in the year 1533" → verbatim "we abide in like manner by the simplicity of our Christian faith [comprised in the Creed], to which Dr. Luther in his sermon, which was delivered in the castle at Torgau in the year 1533, concerning the descent of Christ to hell, has pointed us" (source: https://bookofconcord.org/solid-declaration/christs-descent-into-hell/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/arguments/descent-clause.html: quoted the Old Roman Symbol from Wikipedia as "… crucified under Pontius Pilate and buried; the third day he rose again from the dead …" and dated Marcellus c. 340 → Wikipedia's wording "Who under Pontius Pilate was crucified and buried, on the third day rose again from the dead" and "340 or 341" (source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Roman_Symbol)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/thomas-aquinas.html: Notes quoted On the Angelic Salutation as "was conceived in original [sin], but not born [in it]… cleansed in the womb", splicing §8 before §7 behind an ellipsis and changing the translation's parentheses to brackets → the two phrases quoted separately in text order: "was cleansed in the womb" (§7) and "the Blessed Virgin was conceived in original (sin), but not born (in it)" (§8) (source: Aquinas, On the Angelic Salutation, Writings-Database "Thomas Aquinas/On the Angelic Salutation.html")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/sophronius-of-jerusalem.html: said "the Immaculate" in the Annunciation homily renders the Greek epithet Achrantos → the Greek is tēn amolynton, "the undefiled" (source: Jugie, L'Immaculée Conception… tradition orientale, 1952, pp. 99–101, archive.org, quoting Oratio in Annuntiationem 43, PG 87.3, 3273 D; same Greek in Wikipedia "Immaculate Conception", revision 839627065 of 4 May 2018)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/sophronius-of-jerusalem.html (fn 8, formerly 6): credited Kimel's Eclectic Orthodoxy post "Mary Prokathartheisa" (2015) with Sophronius as "the first point of departure for more fully commenting on Nazianzen's meaning" and the Mary-over-John reading; the post names neither Sophronius nor that phrase → phrase and reading recited to Wikipedia "Immaculate Conception" revision 839627065 (4 May 2018); Kimel cited only for his remark on "the logic driving Kappes's analysis"; Kappes kept as ⚠ unverified (source: afkimel.wordpress.com/2015/09/10/mary-prokathartheisa/; en.wikipedia.org oldid 839627065)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/william-of-ware.html: said Ware's reply to the Damascene objection argues that a preservation can be called a purifying "just as Christ is said to be 'purified' though he had no sin" → the reply makes no such comparison; it distinguishes purgation as removal of uncleanness or as addition of a perfection, says the Damascene means the second, or else purgation from sin that would have been present but for preservation (source: Quaestio Gulielmi Guarrae, Quaracchi 1904, p. 11, archive.org/details/frgulielmiguarra00will)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/william-of-ware.html: "the tomb epitaph naming him Praeceptor Scoti" implied Ware's tomb → the inscription "Magister Gulielmus Varro Præceptor Scoti" is recorded by Wadding on Scotus's tomb at Cologne (source: Paschal Robinson, "William of Ware," Catholic Encyclopedia 1912, newadvent.org/cathen/15640a.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/university-of-paris-oath.html: quoted Grave nimis as "not yet been decided by the Roman Church and the Apostolic See" → "not yet decided by the Roman Church and the Apostolic See", the wording of the translation cited on the Sixtus IV page (source: catholicheritage.blogspot.com/2011/12/immaculate-conception-ii-grave-nimis.html; Denzinger 735 at sensusfidelium.com renders it differently and is noted in the footnote)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation.html: said Origen, last in the eleventh canon's list, is "the earliest of the seven by about three centuries" (Arius, the next earliest, is from the 320s, Origen from the 230s) → "the earliest of the seven: the others run from the 320s to the 440s, Origen from the 230s" (source: the topic's own second-council-of-constantinople.html and the council's list in Tanner, https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum05.htm; same error upstream)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation.html and origen-condemnation/second-council-of-constantinople.html: stated that the council "is known from a Latin version" with the Greek acts lost, omitting that the anathemas survive also in Greek → the minutes survive in Latin, the Greek acts being lost, while the anathemas survive also in Greek, with Origen's name (source: Tanner, introduction, https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum05.htm, "from the Greek text, since this is the more authoritative version"; Pravoslavnaya Entsiklopediya, "Ориген", https://pravenc.ru/text/%D0%9E%D1%80%D0%B8%D0%B3%D0%B5%D0%BD.html, ACO IV.1 p. 242; Ramelli, Brill EEC 2019 PDF, "the original Greek text of the acts of this council is lost")
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/justinian.html: called Butterworth's 1936 translation "the De Principiis every English reader had for the next eighty years", though Crombie's ANF IV translation of Rufinus was available throughout → "the standard English version for some eighty years" (the timeline's wording); and dated Rufinus's Latin Peri Archon "398", contradicting the timeline and other pages (397) → 397 (source: Rufinus's preface to Peri Archon dated "a.d. 397", NPNF2 III, https://www.tertullian.org/fathers2/NPNF2-03/Npnf2-03-35.htm; same wording upstream)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/john-duns-scotus.html: Notes line was garbled ("and that he and that he … through preservative redemptionldquo;removed…") and quoted Holweck as saying Scotus "solved the great difficulty of St. Thomas" → Holweck's words restored: Scotus "removed the great difficulty of St. Thomas" by showing that Mary "obtained of her Divine Son the greatest of redemptions through the mystery of her preservation from all sin" (source: Holweck, "Immaculate Conception," Catholic Encyclopedia, https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07674d.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/arguments/aquinas-denial.html: dated John of Montson's case 1373 without qualification, contradicting the timeline and the Montson page (1387) → "Paris, 1387; dated 1373 by Holweck" (source: Holweck, newadvent.org/cathen/07674d.htm, "(John of Montesono, 1373)"; the John of Montson page's own sources for 1387)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception.html: Disputed-readings link and footnote used the late-development page's old title ("…an unwarranted late development…") → the page's current title, "How does the first-millennium record bear on the 1854 definition of the Immaculate Conception?" (source: the page's own <title>/<h1>)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/immaculate-conception/swiss-reformers.html: said Catholic anthologies cite the "ever chaste, immaculate Virgin" lines from Stakemeier "rather than from a passage of Zwingli's own works" (unsourced judgement; Swan's 2013 post does not discuss Stakemeier) → reports only that they cite Stakemeier, De Mariologia et Oecumenismo (1962), p. 456 (source: http://www.catholicapologetics.info/apologetics/general/mary.htm; https://beggarsallreformation.blogspot.com/2013/02/zwingli-believed-in-immaculate.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/athanasius-of-alexandria.html: said Aquinas quotes the Letter to Epictetus "approvingly … as proof of the descensus" (Summa III q.52) → Aquinas cites it in objection 3 of III q.52 a.2 ("Whether Christ went down into the hell of the lost?") and in his reply prefers Augustine's "better exposition," which does not refer 1 Pet 3:19 to the descent (source: Summa Theologiae III q.52 a.2, https://www.newadvent.org/summa/4052.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/clement-of-alexandria.html: glossed the Cassiodorus fragment on 1 Pet 4:6 as making God's judgments "in this life and the next" remedial and the intermediate state a place of ongoing salvation, and called Clement "the first to build a doctrine on 1 Peter 3:19 + 4:6 as a pair" → the fragment reads "the dead" as "us … who were at one time unbelievers," judged "whilst they are still in the flesh"; gloss rewritten to match and the pairing claim removed (source: Clement, Fragments 1, From the Latin Translation of Cassiodorus, Writings-Database / historicalchristian.faith)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/cyril-of-alexandria.html: glossed "spoiled hell" as "(Greek Hades)" → the Commentary on Luke quoted is R. Payne Smith's translation from the Syriac, the work "had not survived in Greek" apart from catena excerpts; gloss corrected (source: preface to the online edition of Cyril, Commentary on Luke, Writings-Database / historicalchristian.faith)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/benedict-xii.html: said Justin, Irenaeus and Tertullian held that "all souls" wait in a hidden place until the resurrection → Tertullian excepts the martyrs, who pass straight to Paradise; reworded with that exception (source: the page's own sibling tertullian.html, citing Tertullian, A Treatise on the Soul ch. LV)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/justinian.html: said Pope Vigilius "was in Constantinople at the time" (543) and gave his adherence → Vigilius was at Rome (brought to Constantinople in 546, per the topic's council page); edict copies went to the other patriarchs, "including Pope Vigilius", and all gave their adhesion (source: F. Prat, "Origen and Origenism", Catholic Encyclopedia 1911, newadvent.org/cathen/11306b.htm, citing Liberatus and Cassiodorus)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/second-council-of-constantinople.html: Summary said Tanner assigns the fifteen anathemas to a proceeding before the council → Tanner says only they "cannot be attributed to this council"; Summary now gives Diekamp/Price/Florovsky (pre-opening), the Pravoslavnaya Entsiklopediya (lost sitting), Tanner (omitted as not the council's) (source: the page's own fn 7 and fn 9, Tanner introduction at papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum05.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation/arguments/not-condemned-by-ecumenical-council.html: blockquote of canon 11 read "Apollinarius Nestorius" → restored "Apollinarius, Nestorius" (source: Tanner's text of anathema 11 at papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum05.htm as quoted on the topic's timeline, and the page's HEAD text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/origen-condemnation.html: credited Guillaumont, Crouzel and Ramelli alike with holding the condemned propositions not Origen's and Peri Archon written as inquiry → Guillaumont's case limited to the anathemas of 553 describing an Evagrian Origenism (source: the page's own modern-scholarship entries)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/martin-luther.html: Notes said Luther keeps a "real, bodily, post-burial" descent (contradicting the page's own statement that he left body/soul open) → descent of the whole person, God and man, after burial while the body lay in the grave; the sermon says it "did not happen bodily, as Christ surely remained in the grave for the three days" (source: Luther, Third Easter Sermon, Torgau 1533, tr. Rydecki, godwithuslc.org/martin-luthers-sermon-on-christs-descent-into-hell/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/john-damascene.html: heading and notes said the soul is "immortal by grace, not by nature" on the strength of Exposition II.12, which says only that souls are incorporeal "by free gift" ("immortal, not by nature but by grace" is said of angels, II.3) → heading/notes now state the free-gift incorporeality and attribute the grace/immortality line to the angels (source: Writings-Database John Damascene/An Exact Exposition of the Orthodox Faith/Book 2.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/john-damascene.html: quoted Gregory the Dialogist as "kept back … until the Mediator was come" (not his wording) → "kept back by the gates of hell" … "the intercession of the Mediator was not yet come" (source: the blockquote on gregory-the-dialogist.html, Catena Aurea on Luke 16)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon.html: internal contradiction: the Origen entry said Homilies on Joshua 7.1 runs the present twenty-seven books while the intro and Athanasius entry called Festal Letter 39 "the first" catalogue to match the twenty-seven → "first" claim dropped; the Origen entry now attributes the earlier-complete-list view to Kruger, who also notes Athanasius's list is commonly "mentioned as the first time" (source: michaeljkruger.com, "What is the Earliest Complete List of the Canon of the New Testament?", already cited in fn origen-of-alexandria-1)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/thomas-aquinas.html: two quotations cited as "Summa Theologiae, Supplement, Appendix I ('On Purgatory')", but both are in Supplement q. 69 (the linked page), and the purgatory sentence stands in an objection → cited as Supplement q. 69, a. 7, obj. 9 and q. 69, a. 1, ad 3, with Aquinas's reply to obj. 9 and the a. 7 definition quoted (source: https://www.newadvent.org/summa/5069.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/polycarp-of-smyrna.html: page treated it as uncertain whether Polycarp's quotation of Acts 2:24 carries the "Hades" reading, against its own cited ANF note ("Literally, 'having loosed the pains of Hades'"), and said without source that "most manuscripts" of Acts read "death" → Polycarp's Greek reads λύσας τὰς ὠδῖνας τοῦ ᾅδου; the Acts 2:24 witnesses for each reading are reported from the apparatus printed with Bengel's Gnomon (sources: Writings-Database ANF text of the Epistle, note 5; https://greekdoc.com/DOCUMENTS/early/philippians.html; https://biblehub.com/commentaries/acts/2-24.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce.html: Erasmus and Milton entries dated the Westminster Confession 1648 and had it reassert the adultery-and-desertion rule then, contradicting the page's own Westminster entry (completed 1646; the English Parliament's 1648 approval omitted the two divorce paragraphs) → Confession referred to as completed 1646, no 1648 reassertion claim (source: the page's own footnote westminster-confession-3)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/ephrem-the-syrian.html: claimed the Aphrahat page "already notes that in Ephrem 'a similar "sleep of the dead" recurs'", which that page does not say → parenthetical removed (source: docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/aphrahat-the-persian-sage.html, read in full)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/aphrahat-the-persian-sage.html: "soul-sleep of some Anabaptists" set in quotation marks as Calvin's words, but it is not his wording → replaced with his words "maintain that the soul dies or sleeps" and the narrated identification "whom he identifies as some Anabaptists" (source: Calvin, Psychopannychia, preface, Beveridge tr., https://godrules.net/library/calvin/142calvin_c9.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state.html: Aquinas footnote cited the "an abode is assigned … which abode is purgatory" sentence as Suppl. Appendix I (contradicting thomas-aquinas.html) → Suppl. q. 69 a. 7, obj. 9, with the reply's "there must needs be a place where such persons are cleansed" (source: newadvent.org/summa/5069.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/intermediate-state/gregory-the-dialogist.html: quotation "kept back by the gates of hell" left unclosed, running into the narrator's next sentence → closed the quote (source: the page's own block quotation of Gregory, Morals on Job lib. 4 c. 29, via the Catena Aurea)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/altar-ordeal-miracle.html: the sentence "since the story is related only by the Synodicon Vetus, it is not possible to know if it belongs to an older tradition" was attributed to the Phoenix Seminary article → reattributed to Duffy and Parker's edition (n. 36), as quoted by Roger Pearse, with the exact wording; the Phoenix Seminary article does not contain it (source: tertullian.org/rpearse/nicaea.html; ps.edu/council-nicaea-biblical-canon/ via web.archive.org snapshot 2026-02-14)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/altar-ordeal-miracle.html: the Phoenix Seminary article was cited without author or date → John Meade, 25 April 2018 (source: ps.edu/council-nicaea-biblical-canon/ via web.archive.org)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/altar-ordeal-miracle.html: Robert Taylor was said to cite the story "as a weapon against the canon's divine credentials" → Taylor reports it as what "Pappus, in his Synodicon to the council of Nice, asserts", and in the next entry names Laodicea, not Nicaea, as supposed to have given the first catalogue; characterization replaced by his words (source: Taylor, The Diegesis, 1829, p. 432, archive.org full text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/hippo-nicaea-heading.html: Hefele's report of Caecilian's copy of the Nicene canons at Carthage was cited to vol. 1, p. 361 → p. 356 (source: Hefele, A History of the Councils of the Church, vol. 1, archive.org ahistoryofthecou00hefeuoft full text)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/arguments/malachi-2-16-rendering.html: the 2024 article "Does God Really Hate Divorce? A Comparative Analysis of Ancient Texts of Mal 2:14-16" was cited as *HTS Teologiese Studies* 80 (2024), unnamed author → Obedben Mmesomachukwu Lumanze, *Old Testament Essays* 37.2 (2024) (source: the cited SciELO page, scielo.org.za pid S1010-99192024000200001, masthead 'Old Testament Essays… vol.37 n.2 Pretoria 2024')
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/arguments/malachi-2-16-rendering.html: Jerome's Commentary on Malachi quoted as 'When you have hatred, let it go [dimitte]… but iniquity will cover its clothing', LXX 'he will cover your thoughts of wickedness', and 'when we hate our wives, we should divorce them… his garment of iniquity will cover him', none of which is the wording of the cited text → replaced with the cited translation verbatim ('Since you have hated, put away, says the Lord God of Israel; but iniquity shall cover his garment'; 'impiety shall cover your thoughts'; 'when we have hated our wives, we should put them away'; 'iniquity shall cover his garment') (source: Writings-Database Jerome/Commentary on Malachi.html; Commentaries-Database Jerome/Malachi 2_16.toml)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/arguments/malachi-2-16-rendering.html: said the ESV committee revised its text to the man-subject reading in 2016 → the ESV's first printing (2001) already read 'For the man who hates and divorces…' (source: C. J. Collins, 'Malachi 2:16 Again' (2005), p. 13, esvmedia PDF; A. Branch, Midwestern Journal of Theology 10.1 (2011), pp. 72, 80)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/arguments/matthean-exception-source.html: attributed to Fitzmyer (TS 1976, 208, 213-216) the corollary that an exceptionless saying made Jesus stricter than any first-century Jewish school, Matthew's clause aligning him with Shammai; Fitzmyer instead cites the Qumran Temple Scroll and Damascus Document as Palestinian Jews who opposed divorce, supplying 'an intelligible matrix' for an absolute saying, and says Matthew cast the debate 'in terms of the Hillel-Shammai dispute' → restated as Fitzmyer's own reading (source: Fitzmyer, 'The Matthean Divorce Texts', Theological Studies 37 (1976), pp. 206, 222-223, theologicalstudies.net PDF)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/arguments/matthean-exception-source.html: Fitzmyer's note on Vawter's list of non-exceptive readings cited as '208 n. 39' → 207 n. 39 (source: same PDF, note 39 printed before the p. 208 running head)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/martin-luther.html: said Eck branded Luther "a Hussite" at the Leipzig Disputation of 1519 (unsourced quotation) → Eck called him a Hussite in his Obelisci, and at Leipzig (1519) Luther granted some truth in the Hussite opinions; cited inline (source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Eck)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-counted-judith.html: the viva voce reading and the Ephesus/Hebrews parallel were attributed to A. E. Breen → attributed to Hefele, whose text they are; Breen's own words (any "approbation of the book in the deliberations") quoted separately (source: Hefele, A History of the Councils, vol. 1, 2nd ed. 1894, pp. 370–371, archive.org ahistoryofthecou00hefeuoft; Breen, General Introduction, 2nd ed. 1908, p. 361 n., archive.org GeneralIntroToStudyOfHolyScripture)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-counted-judith.html + nicaea-fixed-the-canon.html: Cornely cited as "Introductio specialis in historicos Veteris Testamenti libros (Paris, 1887), p. 24" for a link that opens a different work → "Historicae et criticae introductionis in U.T. libros sacros compendium (Paris: Lethielleux, 1891), pp. 24–25" (source: archive.org historicaeetcri00corngoog metadata and text, pp. 24–25)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-counted-judith.html + nicaea-fixed-the-canon.html: Breen cited as "A General and Critical Introduction to the Study of Holy Scripture (1897)" for a scan of a different edition → "A General Introduction to the Study of Holy Scripture, 2nd ed. (Rochester, 1908)" (source: title page of archive.org GeneralIntroToStudyOfHolyScripture)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-counted-judith.html: the "among the Hagiographa … judged less appropriate" rendering and "is found by the Nicene Council" were attributed to Andrew S. Jacobs's tertullian.org translation → they are Kevin P. Edgecomb's 2006 translation; Jacobs reads "among the apocrypha … deemed less than sufficient" and "is considered to have counted" (source: tertullian.org/fathers/jerome_preface_judith.htm; Writings-Database Jerome/Biblical Prefaces/Judith.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-counted-judith.html: Catholic Answers article quoted anonymously as "a modern Catholic treatment", its sentence cut at "in St. Jerome's day" → author named (Jimmy Akin, 2013) and the sentence quoted in full, "…and that he, himself, believed it" (source: catholic.com/magazine/online-edition/the-first-ecumenical-council-on-the-book-of-judith)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/luther-removed-nt-books.html: the Themelios article "The 'Epistle of Straw'" was attributed to Andrew Naselli and cited for the 1530 Revelation preface → author is Martin Foord, and the article does not discuss Revelation; the 1530 preface re-sourced to the Luther's Works notes and Gieschen, CTQ 79 (2015) 288 n. 26 (source: thegospelcoalition.org/themelios article page; bible-researcher.com/antilegomena.html; ctsfw.net Gieschen PDF). The same misattribution stands on docs/doctrines/canon/martin-luther.html (not edited in this run)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/luther-removed-nt-books.html: "I cannot include him among the chief books, though I would not thereby prevent anyone…" was given as the 1522 preface → that wording is the 1530 revision; 1522 reads "Therefore, I will not have him in my Bible to be numbered among the true chief books, though I would not thereby prevent anyone…" (source: Luther's Works vol. 35 text and notes 5–6 at bible-researcher.com/antilegomena.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/infancy-gospels-suppressed.html: the NASSCAL e-Clavis Protevangelium entry was attributed to Lily C. Vuong with the quotation "attesting to its popularity throughout the Christian world" → the entry is by Eric M. Vanden Eykel and contains no such words; replaced with its "The Greek text of the Protevangelium exists in a daunting ca. 140 MSS" (source: nasscal.com/e-clavis-christian-apocrypha/protevangelium-of-james/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/infancy-gospels-suppressed.html: Protevangelium said to be translated into "a dozen languages" → the eight versions named (Syriac, Coptic, Armenian, Georgian, Ethiopic, Latin, Arabic, Church Slavonic) (source: Vanden Eykel e-Clavis bibliography)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/infancy-gospels-suppressed.html: Eusebius quoted as "no one belonging to the succession of ecclesiastical writers deemed worthy of mention" → "has deemed worthy of mention in his writings" (source: Eusebius, Church History 3.25.6, NPNF, Writings-Database Eusebius of Caesarea/THE CHURCH HISTORY OF EUSEBIUS/Book 3.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/infancy-gospels-suppressed.html: the bartehrman.com article "Infancy Gospel of Thomas: Why It Was Banned from the Bible" was presented without its author → Marko Marina, Ph.D., 4 August 2025 (source: bartehrman.com/infancy-gospel-of-thomas/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/arguments/nicaea-fixed-the-canon.html: Eusebius's "disputed … but nevertheless … publicly used by many" was cited to Church History 3.25 → the phrase is at 3.31.6 (source: Writings-Database Eusebius of Caesarea/THE CHURCH HISTORY OF EUSEBIUS/Book 3.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/arguments/gelasius-reversal-of-discipline.html: James Megivern (1963) filed under the Manichaean "no judgement on communion under one kind" reading with "his conclusion ... not reported" → removed from Held by; footnote now reports that he grants the Manichaean occasion, contrasts William of Champeaux's position with Gelasius's sacrilege dictum, and concludes the Church never considered one species the ideal; question-section disclosure updated to Toner (1908) and Murphy (1909) (source: Ruff, Pray Tell, 6 Dec 2016, https://praytellblog.com/index.php/2016/12/06/more-on-communion-under-both-forms-and-concomitance-in-manchester-new-hampshire/)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/leo-the-great.html: said Toner and Murphy (Catholic Encyclopedia) both read Leo's sermon and Gelasius's decree as anti-Manichaean measures; Murphy's article does not discuss Leo → Toner reads both texts, Murphy reads Gelasius's decree (source: https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/06406a.htm, https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04175a.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/communion-one-kind/council-of-constance.html: unsourced "The decree was confirmed by Pope Martin V" → Martin V's bull Inter cunctas (22 Feb 1418) q. 18 quoted on the custom "approved by the sacred Council of CONSTANCE", with the Catholic Encyclopedia's note that the extent of his confirmation is disputed (source: Denzinger 668, Deferrari trans., sensusfidelium.com; Catholic Encyclopedia "Council of Constance", https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04288a.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/ambrose-of-milan.html: stated that Erasmus coined the label "Ambrosiaster" → removed; the name is credited to the Maurists by René Hoven (1969), others suggest Franciscus Lucas Brugensis; Erasmus doubted the attribution in 1527 (source: Wikipedia, "Ambrosiaster", opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/ambrosiaster.html: implied Erasmus' doubts "earned" the author the name "Ambrosiaster" → Erasmus doubted the ascription (1527); the name credited to the Maurists (Hoven 1969) or Lucas Brugensis, cited (source: Wikipedia, "Ambrosiaster", opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/athenagoras-of-athens.html: summary called the Plea 33 "the earliest surviving patristic quotation of the divorce logion itself", contradicting the page's own note that Justin (c. 155) had quoted its second half → "earliest extant patristic quotation on this timeline of the first half of the divorce logion" (source: the page's own Notes and the Justin Martyr page)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/augustine-of-hippo.html: "lost neither by divorce nor by adultery" was presented as part of On Marriage and Concupiscence I.10[XI] → located in ch. 19 ("Blessing of Matrimony") and cited as such; the chapter title said to be "in the manuscripts" → attributed to the NPNF translation (source: newadvent.org/fathers/15071.htm, opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/council-in-trullo.html: claimed canon 87 "itself flags the divide" between leaving a living spouse and the second marriage of the widowed → removed; canon 87 does not address widowed remarriage. Also "fixes the same penance whether the offender is the wife who leaves or the husband who dismisses" → restated as the canon's own wording (penance for "they who are such", after the husband's clause) (source: Council in Trullo, canons 3 and 87, NPNF2 vol. 14, newadvent.org/fathers/3814.htm, opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/council-of-elvira.html: said the woman of canon 10 "draws Canon 8's deathbed penalty" (canon 8 denies communion even at death) → canon 10 admits her to communion "only at the time of her death" (source: the page's own cited translation of canons 8 and 10)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/council-of-trent.html: called the unconsummated marriage ended by religious profession "the single case" / "the one recognized dissolution of a valid marriage in Western canon law" → removed; the Catholic Code of Canon Law (1983) also provides for papal dissolution of a non-consummated marriage (can. 1142) and the Pauline privilege (can. 1143) (source: vatican.va, CIC Book IV, opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/gallic-councils.html: "simply translates the exception clause of Matthew 19:9 into ecclesiastical law" was presented as Hunter's observation, without his brackets → attributed to Philip Lyndon Reynolds as quoted by Hunter, with Hunter's bracketed "[the exception clause of]" restored (source: Hunter, Vergentis 6 (2018), p. 63, PDF opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/augustine-of-hippo.html: Pollentius called "the layman" → description dropped; Hunter (2018) calls him "an otherwise unknown correspondent… probably a bishop or presbyter", now reported (source: Hunter, Vergentis 6 (2018), p. 56, PDF opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/african-councils.html: quoted "this be made known also to our brother and fellow-priest Boniface" and the "Church beyond the sea" as the closing of the 419 canon 24 → the 397 text carries "Let this be made known also to our brother and fellow-priest Boniface" and "let the church over the sea be consulted to confirm this canon"; canon 24 of 419 reads "Let this be sent to our brother and fellow bishop, Boniface, and to the other bishops of those parts" (source: Code of Canons of the African Church, canon XXIV, NPNF2 vol. 14, ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf214.xv.iv.iv.xxv.html; Wikipedia "Councils of Carthage" §Synod of 397, both opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/african-councils.html: called Hippo canon 36 "the earliest list of the biblical books promulgated by a church council", contradicting the Laodicea witness (canon 60, c. 363) → "the earliest extant conciliar list of the twenty-seven-book New Testament", noting Laodicea's disputed canon 60 (source: the page's own cited Metzger pp. 237-238 and the Laodicea page's Percival/Westcott citations)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/athanasius-of-alexandria.html: called Letter 39 "the first [catalogue] on record that matches the present twenty-seven exactly", contradicting the Origen page's Homilies on Joshua 7.1 enumeration → reports Michael J. Kruger that Athanasius's list is commonly "mentioned as the first time this happened" and that Origen's list came "more than a hundred years before Athanasius" (source: Kruger, "What is the Earliest Complete List of the Canon of the New Testament?", michaeljkruger.com, opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/clement-of-alexandria.html: "fourth-century Codex Alexandrinus" → fifth-century (source: Wikipedia "Codex Alexandrinus", "dated to the fifth century", opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/martin-luther.html: footnote ascribed "The 'Epistle of Straw': Reflections on Luther and the Epistle of James" (Themelios) to Andrew Naselli → Martin Foord, Themelios 45.2; and said the "epistle of straw" paragraph "appears only in the original 1522 edition" → Foord: the declaration "was removed from this preface in all editions of his German Bible after 1537" (source: the article at thegospelcoalition.org/themelios, opened 2026-11-24)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/justin-martyr.html: "Justin is the earliest block on the canon timeline" contradicted the timeline, which places Marcion (c. 144) first → placement note corrected (source: docs/doctrines/canon.html timeline order)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/muratorian-fragment.html: summary quoted "some of us are not willing that it be read in church" and said the Shepherd may be read "privately" → "that the latter be read in church", and "privately" dropped (the text says "it ought indeed to be read; but it cannot be read publicly") (source: the page's own cited Metzger translation, bible-researcher.com/muratorian.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/jerome.html: summary said Jerome "nowhere cites a Roman or Nicene conciliar canon", contradicting the page's own note on his Preface to Judith ("found by the Nicene Council to have been counted among the number of the Sacred Scriptures") → scoped to Letter 129, which names no council (source: Jerome, Letter 129 §3, NPNF2 vol. 6, local text; the page's own Judith note)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/justin-martyr.html: summary said those twice married after divorce are "in the eye of our Master" adulterers; the quoted First Apology 15 says "sinners" → summary now reads "sinners" (source: Justin, First Apology 15, ANF vol. 1, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0126.htm, as quoted on the page)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/justinian.html: summary and notes presented "accommodation with the powers-that-be" as a concession by oikonomia's defenders (Meyendorff) about the practice in general; Meyendorff applies the phrase to "the politically minded patriarchs" in the moechian controversy (795), contrasting the monks' view → reported as Meyendorff's description of that dispute, with his "divorce was a purely civil procedure" statement quoted (source: J. Meyendorff, "Christian Marriage in Byzantium," Dumbarton Oaks Papers 44 (1990), pp. 101-103, https://jbburnett.com/resources/meyendorff_Christian-Marriage-in-Byzantium.pdf)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/lactantius.html: Gregory of Nazianzus quoted as "the men made the laws... I do not accept this legislation" (not verbatim) → "I do not accept this legislation; I do not approve this custom. They who made the Law were men" (source: Gregory of Nazianzus, Oration 37, NPNF2 vol. 7, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/310237.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/martin-luther.html: text said the third ground in The Estate of Marriage rests on the "same Pauline text" as desertion (1 Cor 7:15), contradicting the page's own footnote 6 (1 Cor 7:4-5) → now 1 Corinthians 7:4-5 (source: the page's cited LW 45 text, footnote 6)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/martin-luther.html: Luther and Melanchthon's counsel to Philip of Hesse dated "1540" (unsourced) → advice 10 December 1539, marriage to Margarethe von der Saale 4 March 1540 (source: "Philip I, Landgrave of Hesse," Wikipedia, https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_I,_Landgrave_of_Hesse)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/thomas-aquinas.html: notes said Augustine "retracted" the spiritual-fornication reading of the exception clause, while the Augustine page (Retractations quotation) shows he qualified it → "qualified"; also Aquinas's own treatment now quoted from Supplement q. 62 a. 1 ad 3 (source: https://www.newadvent.org/summa/5062.htm; docs/doctrines/divorce/augustine-of-hippo.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/thomas-aquinas.html: summary said husband and wife are bound "alike" in divorce; Supplement q. 62 a. 4 adds that "they are not judged on a par with each other in reference to those things, since the reason for divorce is greater in one spouse than in the other" → qualification quoted (source: https://www.newadvent.org/summa/5062.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/image-veneration.html: intro glossed image-veneration as "Latin dulia", but Aquinas treats the image of Christ as adored with the same movement as Christ (latria, relatively) → gloss replaced with the Second Council of Nicaea's own term, proskynēsis, "honourable reverence" (source: ST III q.25 a.3, https://www.newadvent.org/summa/4025.htm; Decree of Nicaea II, https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3819.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/image-veneration/arguments/books-of-the-illiterate.html: dated the Libri Carolini "c. 790–791" while the timeline and the Frankfurt page give "c. 790–793" → harmonised to c. 790–793, keeping Ann Freeman's 790–791 drafting date (as reported by Stella) in the footnote (source: the page's own n. 3 and Stella, https://doi.org/10.1017/S1062798722000308; Shahan, https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/03371b.htm, gives 790–92)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/rejection-lists.html: placed "the Gospel of Thomas" and "the Circuit (or Travels) of Thomas" (with the circuits of Paul, Peter and John, the Didache, Clement, Ignatius, Polycarp and Hermas) in the apocrypha of the List of Sixty Books; that list names no Thomas title, and those entries belong to the Stichometry of Nicephorus → reassigned to the Stichometry and the Sixty Books apocrypha given as listed (source: ntcanon.org "Catalogue of the Sixty Canonical Books" and "The Stichometry of Nicephorus", both opened)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/rejection-lists.html: blockquote rendered the tags on the three Decretum V entries as "apocryphal"; Pearse's translation reads "apocryphum" / "apocryphus" / "apocryphus" → quotation corrected (source: tertullian.org/decretum_eng.htm, opened)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/rejection-lists.html: footnote attributed the over-long "Gospel of Thomas" line-count and doubt about its identity to the Wikipedia "Stichometry of Nicephorus" article, which does not discuss it → re-sourced to ntcanon.org's Stichometry page and the Wikipedia "Infancy Gospel of Thomas" article (Kaiser 2025, Burke 2017) (sources opened)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/westminster-confession.html: said Trent's canon 7 anathematized "the claim that the bond of matrimony cannot be dissolved on account of adultery" (inverted: the canon anathematizes anyone who says the Church errs in teaching that) → restated correctly and cited (source: papalencyclicals.net, Trent Session 24, canon 7)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/westminster-confession.html: §6 blockquote and fn quoted the cited OPC edition as "wilful desertion ... by the church or civil magistrate ... own wills and discretion" → OPC text verbatim "willful desertion ... by the church, or civil magistrate ... own wills, and discretion" (source: https://www.opc.org/wcf.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce/westminster-confession.html: Spelling note said the OPC edition prints "wilful" and that ch. 24 stands unchanged in the American text → OPC prints "willful" (Reformed Wiki prints "wilful"); 1887 amendment removed the last sentence of 24.4, §§5–6 unchanged; 1788 revisions specified as 20.4, 23.3, 31.2 (sources: https://www.opc.org/wcf.html, https://reformedwiki.com/confessions/westminster/24, https://opc.org/preface.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon.html: rejection-lists entry and its footnote said the Greek List of Sixty Books and the Stichometry of Nicephorus both reject a "Gospel of Thomas" and a "Circuit of Thomas" (contradicting the rejection-lists page) → the List of Sixty names no Thomas title and no infancy gospel; the Stichometry lists a "Gospel of Thomas" (1,300 lines) and a "Circuit of Thomas" (source: ntcanon.org/Sixty_Canonical_Books.shtml and ntcanon.org/Stichometry_of_Nicephorus.shtml)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/canon/martin-luther.html: blockquote and note 4 gave the 1530 wording of the James preface ("Therefore I cannot include him among the chief books…") labelled as 1522, and printed "apostle, and my reasons follow" → 1522 wording verbatim ("Therefore, I will not have him in my Bible to be numbered among the true chief books… for there are otherwise many good sayings in him"; "apostle; and my reasons follow"), with the 1530 replacement quoted and dated; Dates line updated (source: bible-researcher.com/antilegomena.html, LW 35 text and editors' notes 5–6)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce.html: Erasmus entry called his 1516 Greek New Testament the "first printed" (the Complutensian NT was printed in 1514, published 1520–22) → "first published" (source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complutensian_Polyglot_Bible)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/divorce.html: Erasmus entry said he knew Ambrosiaster "like everyone before the nineteenth century, under the name of Ambrose", contradicting divorce/ambrosiaster.html → "whose commentary circulated under Ambrose's name (Erasmus himself questioned the ascription in 1527)" (source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambrosiaster, already cited on divorce/ambrosiaster.html)

- 2026-11-24 doctrines/ministerial-priesthood/john-chrysostom.html: Notes said the Treatise on the Priesthood (dated on the page c. 386–392, timeline c. 392) was written "before his ordination to the priesthood at Antioch", contradicting the page's own dating → dropped that clause; "written c. 386–392 (the timeline anchors it at c. 392)" (source: the page's own Dates/Summary lines and timeline entry)
- 2026-11-24 doctrines/ministerial-priesthood/leo-the-great.html (+ timeline entry): "handed on in perpetuity" was presented as Leo's words, but it is the NPNF translator's section heading; "the people's choice" glossed a phrase Leo does not use → attributed the phrase to the NPNF heading and used Leo's own "not the prerogative of earthly origin" (source: the page's own fn. 1 and quoted Sermon 3, NPNF 2.12 / newadvent.org/fathers/360303.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/original-sin.html (Council of Trent entry): quotations did not match the cited Waterworth translation ("not by imitation, but by propagation"; "the tinder of sin"; "for the struggle"; "cannot injure those who do not consent"; "lost… holiness and justice") → re-quoted verbatim from Waterworth ("transfused into all by propagation, not by imitation"; "concupiscence, or an incentive (to sin); which, whereas it is left for our exercise, cannot injure those who consent not"; "immediately lost the holiness and justice wherein he had been constituted") (source: https://www.papalencyclicals.net/council-of-trent/fifth-session.htm)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/original-sin.html (Second Council of Orange entry and footnote): "by propagation, not by imitation" attributed to Orange is not in canons 1–2 (it is Trent's phrase); "in body and soul" and "death of the body, which is the punishment of sin" misquoted → phrase removed from the Orange entry; re-quoted "the whole man, that is, both body and soul, that was 'changed for the worse'" and "the death of the body which is the punishment for sin" (source: http://www.crivoice.org/creedorange.html)
- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/original-sin.html (Council of Carthage 418 entry): canon quotations altered ("would have died… whether he had sinned or not sinned"; "derive from Adam original sin" without the canon's "no"; "in a true and not a false sense") → re-quoted "whether he had sinned or not, he would have died in body", "derive from Adam no original sin, which needs to be removed by the laver of regeneration", "is to be understood as false and not true" (source: https://www.earlychurchtexts.com/public/carthage_canons_on_sin_and_grace.htm)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/predestination/augustine-of-hippo.html (retraction section): said Augustine's superseded "small works" included the first book To Simplician (c. 394–396), where he still made faith man's own; Augustine says the opposite — the question was "solved in accordance with the truth" in the first book to Simplicianus, written "in the very beginning of my episcopate" → reworded to the Exposition of Certain Propositions from Romans as the superseded work and To Simplicianus as where he dates the change, with the text quoted (source: Augustine, On the Predestination of the Saints I.7–8 [III–IV], NPNF I.5, Writings-Database Augustine of Hippo/A Treatise on the Predestination of the Saints/Book 1.html)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/predestination.html (timeline order): the Arminius/Remonstrance/Dort entry (1610–1619) stood after the Jansen entry (1640–1653) → moved before it, earliest first (source: the page's own entry dates)

- 2026-11-24 docs/doctrines/predestination/gottschalk-of-orbais.html: Valence (855) "led, by the usual account, by Remigius of Lyon and Florus" (⚠ unverified, no holder) → "with Remigius of Lyon presiding", Florus dropped from the leadership claim (source: Wikipedia, "Remigius of Lyon": "presided over the Synod of Valence in 855")

- 2026-11-25 doctrines/indulgences/martin-luther.html: 'denying any papal power over the guilt of sin or over the punishments God reserves' misstated thesis 6, and the power over penalties was cited to 'theses 5–6' → penalties 'which he has imposed either by his own authority or by that of the Canons' (thesis 5); guilt remitted only 'by declaring that it has been remitted by God,' though 'he may grant remission in cases reserved to his judgment' (thesis 6); summary now cites thesis 5 (source: Ninety-Five Theses 5–6, projectwittenberg.org)
- 2026-11-25 doctrines/ministerial-priesthood/jerome.html (both Titus blockquotes, summary, Notes, nn. 1, 3; also doctrines/ministerial-priesthood.html Jerome entry and n. 43, and doctrines/church-government/arguments/bishop-presbyter-one-office.html blockquote, prose and n. 1): Titus 1:5–7 quoted from a translation other than the one cited, dropping 'more by custom than by the truth of the Lord's arrangement' → restored verbatim: 'A presbyter, therefore, is the same as a bishop… it was decreed throughout the whole world that one chosen from among the presbyters should be set over the rest…'; 'let the bishops know that they are greater than the presbyters more by custom than by the truth of the Lord's arrangement' (source: Writings-Database Jerome/Commentary on Titus.html)
- 2026-11-25 scripts/ledger/index-paragraphs/ministerial-priesthood.html: 'The same sources speak of the priesthood of all the baptized' (Trent, Hopko, the Apology), though Trent sess. 23 ch. 4 condemns the claim 'that all Christians indiscrimately are priests of the New Testament' → 'Leo the Great and Lumen Gentium, among the witnesses, also speak of the priesthood of the whole baptized people' (source: papalencyclicals.net, Trent session 23, ch. 4; the timeline intro)
- 2026-11-25 doctrines/ministerial-priesthood/clement-of-rome.html: 'Ignatius, Justin, Cyprian… use sacerdotal terms', though Justin's president is not called 'priest' and Ignatius does not call a Christian minister hiereus → Ignatius ties the proper Eucharist to the bishop, Justin describes a presiding 'president', Cyprian calls the celebrant sacerdos (source: the Justin, Ignatius and Cyprian pages on this timeline; First Apology 65, 67; Cyprian, Ep. 62)
